Linear ℤ 𝟐 𝒏 \boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}} -Manifolds and Linear Actions
Andrew James BRUCE, Eduardo IBARGUËNGOYTIA and Norbert PONCIN

Abstract
We establish the representability of the general linear ℤ 2 n {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n} -group and use the restricted functor of points – whose test category is the category of ℤ 2 n {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n} -manifolds over a single topological point – to define its smooth linear actions on ℤ 2 n {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n} -graded vector spaces and linear ℤ 2 n {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n} -manifolds. Throughout the paper, particular emphasis is placed on the full faithfulness and target category of the restricted functor of points of a number of categories that we are using.



keywords
supergeometry; ringed spaces; functors of points; linear group actions

AMS
58A50; 58C50; 14A22; 14L30; 13F25; 16L30; 17A70

1
Introduction

In order to be able to deal with the technical details of vector bundles and related structures in the category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds (for
n=1
see
[
6
]
), we need some foundational results on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Lie groups and their smooth linear actions on linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds. However, the proofs of some folklore results, i.e., results that we tended to accept somewhat hands-waving, are often not at all obvious in the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-context. The present paper, beyond its supposed applications, intrinsic interest and the beauty of some of its developments, raises the question of the scientific value of “results” that are partially based on speculations.

Loosely speaking,
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds (
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\times n}
) are “manifolds” for which the structure sheaf has a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-grading and the commutation rules for the local coordinates comes from the standard scalar product (see
[
11
,
13
,
14
,
15
,
18
,
19
,
20
,
21
,
37
]
for details). This is not just a trivial or straightforward generalization of the notion of a standard supermanifold, as one has to deal with formal coordinates that anticommute with other formal coordinates, but are themselves
not
nilpotent. Due to the presence of formal variables that are not nilpotent, formal power series are used rather than polynomials. Recall that for standard supermanifolds all functions are polynomial in the Grassmann odd variables. The theory of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-geometry is currently being developed and many foundational questions remain. For completeness, we include Appendix
B
in which the foundations of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-geometry are given. In this paper, we examine the relation between
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces and linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds, and then we use this to define linear actions of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Lie groups.

In the literature on supergeometry, the symbol
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|q}
has two distinct, but related meanings. First, we have the notion of a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded, or super, vector space with
p
even and
q
odd dimensions, i.e., the real vector space
\mathbf{R}^{p|q}={\mathbb{R}}^{p}\bigoplus{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
. Secondly, we have the locally ringed space
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|q}=\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p},C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}[\mathchar 28952\relax]\big)
, where
\mathchar 28952\relax^{i}
(
i\in\{1,\dots,q\}
) are the generators of a Grassmann algebra. The difference can be highlighted by identifying the points of these objects. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-graded vector space has as its underlying topological space
{\mathbb{R}}^{p+q}
(we just forget the “superstructure”), while for the locally ringed space the topological space is
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
. There are several ways of showing that these two notions are deeply tied. In particular, the category of finite dimensional super vector spaces is equivalent to the category of “linear supermanifolds” (see
[
8
,
32
,
33
,
34
,
38
]
).

In this paper, we will show that the categories of finite dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces
\mathbf{V}
and linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
V
are isomorphic. We do this by explicitly constructing a “manifoldification” functor
\mathcal{M}
, which associates a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold to every finite dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space, and a “vectorification” functor, which is the inverse of the previous functor. It turns out that working in a coordinate-independent way
(\mathbf{V},V)
is much more complex than working with canonical coordinates
\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)
.

Throughout this article, a special focus is placed on functors of points. The functor of points has been used informally in physics as from the very beginning. It is actually of importance in contexts where there is no good notion of point as in super- and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-geometry and in algebraic geometry. For instance, homotopical algebraic geometry
[
42
,
43
]
and its generalisation that goes under the name of homotopical algebraic geometry over differential operators
[
25
,
26
]
, are completely based on the functor of points approach. In this paper, we are particularly interested in functors of
\mathchar 28675\relax
-points, i.e., functors of points from appropriate locally small categories
\tt C
to a functor category whose source is not the category
{\tt C}^{\operatorname{op}}
but the category
{\tt G}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebras
\mathchar 28675\relax
. However, functors of points that are restricted to the very simple test category
{\tt G}
are fully faithful only if we replace the target category of the functor category by a subcategory of the category of sets.

More precisely, closely related to the above isomorphism of supervector spaces and linear supermanifolds is the so-called “even rules”. Loosely this means including extra odd parameters to render everything even and in doing so one removes copious sign factors (see for example
[
24
, Section 1.7]
). We will establish an analogue of the even rules in our higher graded setting which we will refer to as the “zero degree rules” (see Definition
2.1
). To address this we will make extensive use of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebras
\mathchar 28675\relax
,
\Lambda
-points and the Schwarz–Voronov embedding, which is a fully faithful functor of points
\mathcal{S}
from
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds to a functor category with source
{\tt G}
and the category of Fréchet manifolds (see for
example
[
30
]
) over commutative Fréchet algebras as target (see
[
13
]
). We show that the zero degree rules functor
\mathcal{F}
, understood as an assignment of a functor from
{\tt G}
to the category of modules over commutative (Fréchet) algebras, given a (finite dimensional)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space, is fully faithful (see Theorem
2.2
and Proposition
2.25
). The “zero degree rules” allow one to identity a finite dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space, considered as a functor, with the functor of points of its “manifoldification”. In other words, the composite
\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{F}
can be viewed as functors between the same categories and are naturally isomorphic. This identification is fundamental when describing linear group actions on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces and linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds.

Another important part of this work is the category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Lie groups and its fully faithful functor of points valued in a functor category with
{\tt G}
as source category and Fréchet Lie groups over commutative Fréchet algebras as target category. We define the general linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-group as a functor in this functor category and show that it is representable, i.e., is a genuine
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold (see Theorem
3.4
). This leads to interesting insights into the computation of the inverse of an invertible degree zero
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded square matrix of dimension
p|\underline{q}
with entries in a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative algebra. Furthermore, the approach using
\Lambda
-points and the zero rules allows us to construct a canonical smooth linear action of the general linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-group on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces and linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds. All these notions, in particular the equivalence between the definitions of a smooth linear action as natural transformation and as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism, are carefully and explicitly explained in the main text.

We remark that many of the statements in this paper are not surprising in themselves. However, due to the subtleties of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-geometry, many of the proofs are much more involved than the analogue statements in supergeometry. The main source of difficulty is that one has to deal with formal power series in non-zero degree coordinates, rather than polynomials as one does in supergeometry. This forces one to work with infinite dimensional objects and the
\mathcal{J}
-adic topology (
\mathcal{J}
is the ideal generated by non-zero degree elements). Many of the “categorical” proofs are significantly more involved than the proofs for supermanifolds. In general, there is a lot of work to establish the form of natural transformations as we have non-nilpotent elements of non-zero degree. While the ethos of the proofs may be standard, they are not, in general, simple or routine checks due to the aforementioned subtleties.

Motivation from physics:

{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-gradings (
n\geq 2
) can be found in the theory of parastatistics (see for example
[
27
,
28
,
29
,
48
]
) and in relation to an alternative approach to supersymmetry
[
1
,
2
,
44
]
. “Higher graded” generalizations of the super Schrödinger algebra (see
[
3
]
) and the super Poincaré algebra (see
[
10
]
) have appeared in the literature. Furthermore, such gradings appear in the theory of mixed symmetry tensors as found in string theory and some formulations of supergravity (see
[
12
]
). It must also be pointed out that quaternions and more general Clifford algebras can be understood as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative algebras
[
4
,
5
]
. Finally,
any
“sign rule” can be interpreted in terms of a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-grading (see
[
18
]
).

Background:
For various sheaf-theoretical notions we will draw upon Hartshorne
[
31
, Chapter II]
and Tennison
[
41
]
. There are several good introductory books on the theory of supermanifolds including Bartocci, Bruzzo and Hernández Ruipérez
[
7
]
, Carmeli, Caston and Fioresi
[
16
]
, Deligne and Morgan
[
24
]
and Varadarajan
[
45
]
. For categorical notions we will be based on Mac Lane
[
35
]
. We will make extensive use of the constructions and statements found in our earlier publications
[
13
,
14
,
15
]
.

2
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-graded vector spaces and Linear
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-manifolds

2.1
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-graded vector spaces and the zero degree rules

When dealing with linear superalgebra one encounters the so-called
even rules
(see
[
16
, Section 1.8]
,
[
24
, Section 1.7]
and
[
45
, pp. 123–124]
, for example). Very informally, the idea is to remove sign factors by allowing extra parameters to render the situation completely even. The idea has been applied in physics since the early days of supersymmetry. More precisely, let

V(A)=(A\otimes V)_{0}

be the even part of the extension of scalars in a (real) super vector space
V
, from the base field
{\mathbb{R}}
to a supercommutative algebra
A\in\tt SAlg
(in the even rules that we are about to describe, it actually suffices to use supercommutative Grassmann algebras
A={\mathbb{R}}[\mathchar 28946\relax_{1},\dots,\mathchar 28946\relax_{N}]
: the
\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}
are then the extra parameters mentioned before). The main result in even rules states, roughly, that defining a morphism
\phi\colon V\otimes V\to V
is equivalent to defining it functorially on the even part of
V
after extension of scalars, i.e., is equivalent to defining a functorial family of morphisms

\phi(A)\colon\ V(A)\times V(A)\to V(A)

(indexed by
A\in\tt SAlg
). More precisely, there is a
1:1
correspondence between parity respecting
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear maps
\phi\colon V_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes V_{n}\to V
and functorial families

\phi(A)\colon\ V_{1}(A)\times\dots\times V_{n}(A)\to V(A)

(
A\in\tt SAlg
) of
A_{0}
-multilinear maps.

We now proceed to generalise this theorem to the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-setting. We will work with the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt GrAlg
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebras rather than the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Alg
of all
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative algebras.

Let
{V}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}{V}_{\gamma_{i}}
be a (real)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space, i.e., a (real) vector space with a direct sum decomposition over
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
(we say that the vectors of
V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
are of degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
). The category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces (not necessarily finite dimensional) we denote as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Vec}}
. Morphisms in this category are degree preserving linear maps. We denote the category of modules over commutative algebras as
\tt AMod
(see Appendix
A
).

To
V
we associate a functor

{V}(-)\in{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Pts}}^{\textnormal{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)

in the category of those functors whose value on any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}}
is a
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module, and of those natural transformations that have
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components. The functor
V(-)
is essentially the tensor product functor
-\otimes V
. It is built in the following way. First, for every
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra
\Lambda
, we define

{V}(\Lambda):=(\Lambda\otimes{V})_{0}\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\tt Mod,

where the tensor product is over
{\mathbb{R}}
. Secondly, for any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra morphism
\varphi^{*}\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
, we define

{V}(\varphi^{*}):=\big(\varphi^{*}\otimes\mathbbmss{1}_{{V}}\big)_{0},

where the
RHS
is the restriction of
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\otimes\mathbbmss{1}_{V}
to the degree 0 part of
\mathchar 28675\relax\otimes V
, so that
{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is an
\tt AMod
-morphism

\displaystyle{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\colon\ {V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\to{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}),

(2.1)

whose associated algebra morphism is the restriction
(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})_{0}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}_{0}
. It is clear that
V(-)
respects compositions and identities and is thus a functor, as announced.

We thus get an assignment

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec}\ni V\mapsto\mathcal{F}(V):=V(-)\in{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt AMod\big).

The map
\mathcal{F}
is essentially
-\otimes\bullet
and is itself a functor. It associates to any grading respecting linear map
\phi\colon{V}\rightarrow{W}
and any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax
, a
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear map

\phi_{\Lambda}:=(\mathbbmss{1}_{\Lambda}\otimes\phi)_{0}\colon\ {V}(\Lambda)\rightarrow{W}(\Lambda).

The family
\mathcal{F}(\phi):=\phi_{-}
is a natural transformation from
\mathcal{F}(V)
to
\mathcal{F}(W)
. Since
\mathcal{F}
respects compositions and identities, it is actually a functor valued in the restricted functor category
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt AMod\big)
.

Definition 2.1
.

The functor

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Vec}}\longrightarrow{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Pts}}^{\textnormal{op}},\textnormal{{AMod}}\big)

is referred to as the
zero degree rules functor
.

Theorem 2.2
.

The zero degree rules functor

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Vec}}\longrightarrow{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Pts}}^{\textnormal{op}},\textnormal{{AMod}}\big)

is fully faithful, i.e., for any pair of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces
{V}
and
{W}
, the map

\mathcal{F}_{V,W}\colon\ \Hom_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Vec}}}({V},{W})\longrightarrow\Hom_{{\tt Fun}_{0}({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Pts}}^{\textnormal{op}},\textnormal{{AMod}})}(\mathcal{F}({V}),\mathcal{F}({W})),

is a bijection.

This result is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-counterpart of the
1:1
correspondence mentioned above.

Proof.

We show first that the map
\mathcal{F}_{V,W}
is injective. Let
\phi,\psi\colon V\to W
be two degree preserving linear maps, and assume that
\mathcal{F}(\phi)=\phi_{-}=\psi_{-}=\mathcal{F}(\psi)
, so that, for any
\mathchar 28675\relax\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}}
and any
\mathchar 28949\relax\otimes v\in V(\mathchar 28675\relax)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathchar 28949\relax\otimes\phi(v)=\phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax\otimes v)=\psi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax\otimes v)=\mathchar 28949\relax\otimes\psi(v).

(2.2)

Notice now that

V(\mathchar 28675\relax)=(\mathchar 28675\relax\otimes V)_{0}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\otimes V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}

and let
\mathchar 28675\relax
be the Grassmann algebra

\displaystyle\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}:={\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28946\relax_{1},\dots,\mathchar 28946\relax_{N}]]

(2.3)

that has exactly one generator
\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}
in each non-zero degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
(
N=2^{n}-1
). For any
v_{j}\in V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
, equation (
2.2
) implies that
\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes\mathchar 28958\relax(v_{j})=\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes\psi(v_{j})
, so that
\phi
and
\psi
coincide on
V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
, for all
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
. For
v_{0}\in V_{0}:=V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{0}}
and
\mathchar 28949\relax=1
, the same equation shows that
\phi
and
\psi
coincide also on
V_{0}
.

To prove surjectivity, we consider an arbitrary natural transformation
\Phi_{-}\colon V(-)\to W(-)
and will define a degree 0 linear map
\phi\colon V\to W
, such that
\mathcal{F}(\phi)=\phi_{-}=\Phi_{-}
, i.e., such that, for any
\mathchar 28675\relax\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}=\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}

on
V(\mathchar 28675\relax)
.
Since an element of
V(\mathchar 28675\relax)
(uniquely) decomposes into a sum over
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
of (not uniquely defined) finite sums of decomposable tensors
\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}
, with (not uniquely defined) factors
\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}
and
v_{i}
of degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i})=\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}),

(2.4)

for all
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
.

Further, it suffices to prove condition (
2.4
) for
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
(see (
2.3
)) and for the tensors
\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}
(
\mathchar 28946\relax_{0}:=1
,
v_{i}\in V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
,
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
). The observation follows from naturality of
\Phi
. Indeed, assume that (
2.4
) is satisfied for
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
and the decomposable tensors just mentioned (assumption (
\star
)). For any fixed
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
(resp.,
i=0
), and for
\mathchar 28675\relax
,
\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}
and
v_{i}
as above, let
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}\to\mathchar 28675\relax
be the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra map defined by
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{i})=\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}
,
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j})=0
for
j\neq i
,
j\neq 0
, and
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{0})=\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(1)=1
(resp.,
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j})=0
for all
j\neq 0
, and
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{0})=\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(1)=1
). For
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
, when applying the naturality condition
\textstyle{{V}(\Lambda_{1})}
\textstyle{{W}(\Lambda_{1})}
\textstyle{{V}(\Lambda)}
\textstyle{{W}(\Lambda)}
\textstyle{\Phi_{\Lambda_{1}}}
\textstyle{{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})}
\textstyle{{W}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})}
\textstyle{\Phi_{\Lambda}}

to
\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}
, we get clockwise

W(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})(\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}))=W(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})(\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes\phi(v_{i}))=\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{i})\otimes\phi(v_{i})=\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}\otimes\mathchar 28958\relax(v_{i})=\phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}),

in view of (
\star
), whereas anticlockwise we obtain

\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(V(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})(\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}))=\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}).

Hence, condition (
2.4
) holds for
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
. For
i=0
, the above naturality condition yields
1\otimes\phi(v_{0})=\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(1\otimes v_{0})
, when applied to
1\otimes v_{0}
. In view of the
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linearity of the
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components of the natural transformations considered, we get now

\phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax_{0}\otimes v_{0})=\mathchar 28949\relax_{0}\,\phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(1\otimes v_{0})=\mathchar 28949\relax_{0}(1\otimes\phi(v_{0}))=\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathchar 28949\relax_{0}\otimes v_{0}).

Finally, condition (
2.4
) holds for an arbitrary
\mathchar 28675\relax
, if it holds for
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
.

Surjectivity now reduces to constructing a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
-morphism
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon V\to W
that satisfies (
2.4
) for
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
and decomposable tensors of the type
\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}
(
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
).

We first build
\phi(v_{j})\in W_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
linearly in
v_{j}\in V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
for an arbitrarily fixed
j\in\{0,\dots,N\}
. We set again
\mathchar 28946\relax_{0}=1\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{1,0}
. Since
\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})\in(\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}\otimes W)_{0}
, it reads

\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})=\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{M_{i}}\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}^{k}\otimes w_{i}^{k},

where
M_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}^{k}\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{1,\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
and
w_{i}^{k}\in W_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
. When setting

\mathcal{A}_{i}=\bigg\{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathbb{N}^{\times N}\colon\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\mathchar 28939\relax_{\ell}\mathchar 28941\relax_{\ell}=\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}\bigg\}

and

\mathchar 28949\relax_{i}^{k}=\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}r_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}^{k}\,\mathchar 28946\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\qquad\big(r_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}^{k}\in{\mathbb{R}}\big),

where we used the standard multi-index notation, we get

\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})=\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\mathchar 28946\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\otimes\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{M_{i}}r_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}^{k}w_{i}^{k}\bigg)=:\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}}\mathchar 28946\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\otimes w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}\qquad(w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}\in W_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}).

Denoting the canonical basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
by
(e_{\ell})_{\ell}
and decomposing the
RHS
with respect to the values of
|\mathchar 28939\relax|=\mathchar 28939\relax_{1}+\dots+\mathchar 28939\relax_{N}\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})=w_{0,0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes w_{e_{i},i}+\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\colon|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 2}\mathchar 28946\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\otimes w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}.

(2.5)

Let now
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}}
(
r_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
r_{0}>0
and
r_{0}\neq 1
) be the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra endomorphism of
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
that is defined by
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{k})=r_{0}\mathchar 28946\relax_{k}
if
k\neq 0
and by
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{0})=1
. It follows from the naturality condition
\textstyle{{V}(\Lambda_{1})}
\textstyle{{W}(\Lambda_{1})}
\textstyle{{V}(\Lambda_{1})}
\textstyle{{W}(\Lambda_{1})}
\textstyle{\Phi_{\Lambda_{1}}}
\textstyle{{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}})}
\textstyle{\ {W}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}})}
\textstyle{\Phi_{\Lambda_{1}}}

that

W(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}})(\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j}))=w_{0,0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes(r_{0}w_{e_{i},i})+\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\colon|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 2}\mathchar 28946\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\otimes\big(r_{0}^{|\mathchar 28939\relax|}w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}\big)

and

\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}\big(V(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{r_{0}})(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})\big)=r_{0}^{1-\mathchar 28942\relax_{j0}}w_{0,0}+\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes\big(r_{0}^{1-\mathchar 28942\relax_{j0}}w_{e_{i},i}\big)+\sum_{i=0}^{N}\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\colon|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 2}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathchar 28946\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\otimes\big(r_{0}^{1-\mathchar 28942\relax_{j0}}w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}\big),

where
\mathchar 28942\relax_{j0}
is the Kronecker symbol, coincide. As all the monomials in
\mathchar 28946\relax
in the
RHS
-s of the two last equations are different, we get,

(1)

if
j\neq 0
:
w_{0,0}=0
and
w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}=0
, for all
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
and all
\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\colon|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 2
, and,

(2)

if
j=0
:
w_{e_{i},i}=0
, for all
i\in\{1,\dots,N\}
, and
w_{\mathchar 28939\relax,i}=0
, for all
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
and all
\mathchar 28939\relax\in\mathcal{A}_{i}\colon|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 2
.

Equation (
2.5
) thus yields

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes w_{e_{i},i}\quad(j\neq 0)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(1\otimes v_{0})=w_{00}.

(2.6)

If
j\neq 0
, a new application of naturality, now for the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra endomorphism
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{R_{0}}
(
R_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
R_{0}\neq 1
) of
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
that is defined by
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{R_{0}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{i})=R_{0}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}
(
i\neq 0,i\neq j
),
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{R_{0}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j})=\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}
and
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{R_{0}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{0})=1
, leads to

\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes w_{e_{j},j}+\sum_{i\neq j}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes(R_{0}w_{e_{i},i})=\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes w_{e_{j},j}+\sum_{i\neq j}\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes w_{e_{i},i},

so that

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}}(\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes v_{j})=\mathchar 28946\relax_{j}\otimes w_{e_{j},j}\qquad(j\neq 0).

(2.7)

The vectors
w_{00}\in W_{0}
(see (
2.6
)) and
w_{e_{j},j}\in W_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
(
j\neq 0
) (see (
2.7
)) are well-defined and depend obviously linearly on
v_{0}
and
v_{j}
, respectively. Hence, setting
\phi(v_{0})=w_{00}
and
\phi(v_{j})=w_{e_{j},j}
(
j\neq 0
), we define a degree 0 linear map from
V
to
W
. Moreover, since (
2.4
) is clearly satisfied for
\mathchar 28675\relax_{1}
and the
\mathchar 28946\relax_{i}\otimes v_{i}
(
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
), it is satisfied for any
\mathchar 28675\relax
, which completes the proof of surjectivity.
∎

Since

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt AMod\big)

is fully faithful, it is essentially injective, i.e., it is injective on objects up to isomorphism. It follows that
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec}
can be viewed as a full subcategory of the target category of
\mathcal{F}
.

The above considerations lead to the following definition.

Definition 2.3
.

A functor

\mathcal{V}\in{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{Pts}}^{\textnormal{op}},\textnormal{{AMod}}\big)

is said to be
representable
, if there exists
V\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
, such that
\mathcal{F}(V)
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathcal{V}
.

As
\mathcal{F}
is essentially injective, a
representing object

V
, if it exists, is unique up to isomorphism. We therefore refer sometimes to
V
as “the” representing object.

2.2
Cartesian
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-graded vector spaces and Cartesian
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-manifolds

In the literature, the space
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is viewed, either as the trivial
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold

{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}=\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p},C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]\big)

with canonical
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded formal parameters
\mathchar 28952\relax
, or as the Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space

\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}=\mathbf{R}^{p}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{R}^{q_{j}},

where
\mathbf{R}^{p}
(resp.,
\mathbf{R}^{q_{j}}
) is the term of degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{0}=0\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
(resp.,
\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
). Observe that we use the notation
\mathbf{R}^{\bullet}
(resp.,
{\mathbb{R}}^{\bullet}
), when
{\mathbb{R}}^{\bullet}
is viewed as a vector space (resp., as a manifold). It can happen that we write
{\mathbb{R}}^{\bullet}
for both, the vector space and the manifold, however, in these cases, the meaning is clear from the context. Further, we set
q_{0}=p
,
\mathbf{q}=(q_{0},q_{1},\dots,q_{N})
, and
|\mathbf{q}|=\sum_{i}q_{i}
. When embedding
\mathbf{R}^{q_{i}}
(
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
) into
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
, we identify each vector of the canonical basis of
\mathbf{R}^{q_{i}}
with the corresponding vector of the canonical basis of
\mathbf{R}^{|\mathbf{q}|}
. We denote this basis by

\big(e^{i}_{k}\big)_{i,k}\qquad(i\in I=\{0,\dots,N\},\,k\in K_{i}=\{1,\dots,q_{i}\})

and assign of course the degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}
to every vector
e^{i}_{k}
. We can now write

\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\mathbf{R}^{q_{i}}=\bigoplus_{(i,k)\in I\times K_{i}}\mathbf{R}\,e^{i}_{k}.

The dual space of
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
is defined by

\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathbf{R}\big)=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathbf{R}\big),

where
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}
is the internal Hom of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
, i.e., the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space of all linear maps, and where
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
is the vector space of all degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}
linear maps. We sometimes write
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}
instead of
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}
. The dual basis of
\big(e^{i}_{k}\big)_{i,k}
is defined as usual by

\mathchar 28962\relax^{k}_{i}\big(e^{j}_{\ell}\big)=\mathchar 28942\relax^{j}_{i}\mathchar 28942\relax^{k}_{\ell},

so that
\mathchar 28962\relax^{k}_{i}
is a linear map of degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}
and

\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{(i,k)\in I\times K_{i}}\mathbf{R}\,\mathchar 28962\relax^{k}_{i}.

Let us finally mention that any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector
x\in\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
reads
x=\sum_{j,\ell}x^{\ell}_{j}e^{j}_{\ell}
and that

\displaystyle\mathchar 28962\relax^{k}_{i}(x)=x^{k}_{i},

(2.8)

as usual.

Notice now that if
M
is a smooth
m
-dimensional real manifold and
(U,\mathchar 28967\relax)
is a chart of
M
, the coordinate map
\mathchar 28967\relax
sends any point
x\in M
to
\mathchar 28967\relax(x)=(x^{1},\dots,x^{m})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathchar 28967\relax^{i}(x)=x^{i}.

(2.9)

Hence, what we refer to as coordinate function
x^{i}\in C^{\infty}(U)
is actually the function
\mathchar 28967\relax^{i}
. Equations (
2.8
) and (
2.9
) suggest to associate to any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
with coordinate functions
\mathchar 28962\relax^{k}_{i}
. In other words, the associated
p|\underline{q}
-dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold will be the locally
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-ringed space

{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}=\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}\big)=\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p},C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}[[\mathchar 28962\relax_{1}^{1},\dots,\mathchar 28962\relax_{N}^{q_{N}}]]\big),

where
C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}
is the standard function sheaf of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
, where the degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}
linear maps
\mathchar 28962\relax_{j}^{1},\dots,\mathchar 28962\relax_{j}^{q_{j}}
(
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
) are interpreted as coordinate functions or formal parameters of degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}
, and where the degree
0
linear maps
\mathchar 28962\relax_{0}^{1},\dots,\mathchar 28962\relax_{0}^{p}
are viewed as coordinates in
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
. We often set

\displaystyle\mathchar 28952\relax^{\ell}_{j}:=\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{j}\quad(j\neq 0)\qquad\text{and}\qquad x^{\ell}:=\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{0}.

(2.10)

Remark 2.4
.

In the following, we denote the coordinates of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
by

\big(x^{\ell},\mathchar 28952\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)=\big(x^{a},\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}\big)=(u^{\mathfrak{a}}),

if we wish to make a distinction between the coordinates of degree
0,\mathchar 28941\relax_{1},\dots,\mathchar 28941\relax_{N}
, if we distinguish between zero degree coordinates and non-zero degree ones, or if we consider all coordinates together.

We refer to the category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
(
p,q_{1},\dots,q_{N}\in\mathbb{N}
) and degree 0 linear maps, as the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarVec
of Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces. As just mentioned, the interpretation of the dual basis as coordinates leads naturally to a map

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\ni\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man,

where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man
is the category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds and corresponding morphisms. This map can easily be extended to a functor. Indeed, if
\mathbf{L}\colon\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\to\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}
is a morphism in
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarVec

\big(
it is canonically represented by a block diagonal matrix
\mathbf{L}\in\operatorname{gl}\big(r|\underline{s}\times p|\underline{q},\mathbf{R}\big)\big)
, its dual (
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-transpose)
\mathbf{L}^{\vee}\colon\big(\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}\big)^{\vee}\to\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}

\big(
which is represented by the standard transpose
{}^{t}\mathbf{L}\in\operatorname{gl}\big(p|\underline{q}\times r|\underline{s},\mathbf{R}\big)\big)
is also a degree 0 linear map. If we set

\mathbf{L}=\big(\mathbf{L}^{\ell i}_{ik}\big),

where
i\in I
,
\ell\in\{1,\dots,s_{i}\}
label the row and
i\in I
,
k\in\{1,\dots,q_{i}\}
label the column, we get

\mathbf{L}^{\vee}\big(\mathchar 28962\relax^{\prime\ell}_{i}\big)=\sum_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathbf{L}^{\ell i}_{ik}\mathchar 28962\relax^{k}_{i},

where
\big(\mathchar 28962\relax^{\prime\ell}_{i}\big)_{i,\ell}
is the basis of
\big(\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}\big)^{\vee}
. When using notation (
2.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle L^{*}\big(x^{\prime\ell}\big):=\mathbf{L}^{\vee}\big(x^{\prime\ell}\big)=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\mathbf{L}^{\ell 0}_{0k}\,x^{k}\in\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\qquad(\ell\in\{1,\dots,r\})

(2.11)

and

\displaystyle L^{*}\big(\mathchar 28952\relax^{\prime\ell}_{j}\big):=\mathbf{L}^{\vee}\big(\mathchar 28952\relax^{\prime\ell}_{j}\big)=\sum_{k=1}^{q_{j}}\mathbf{L}^{\ell j}_{jk}\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{k}_{j}\in\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}^{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\qquad(j\neq 0,\,\ell\in\{1,\dots,s_{j}\}).

(2.12)

These pullbacks define a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
L\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
. This is the searched
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})\colon\mathcal{M}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\to\mathcal{M}\big(\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}\big)
. Since
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})
is defined interpreting the standard transpose
{}^{t}\mathbf{L}
as pullback
(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}))^{*}
of coordinates, we have

(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{M}\circ\mathbf{L}))^{*}\simeq\,^{t}\mathbf{L}\circ\,^{t}\mathbf{M}\simeq(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}))^{*}\circ(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{M}))^{*}=(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{M})\circ\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}))^{*},

so that
\mathcal{M}
respects composition. Further, it obviously respects identities. Hence, we defined a functor
\mathcal{M}
.

The pullbacks (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) are actually linear homogeneous
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-functions, i.e., homogeneous
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-functions in

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p}):=\bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{p}r_{k}\,x^{k}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{q_{j}}r^{j}_{k}\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{k}_{j}\colon r_{k},r^{j}_{k}\in{\mathbb{R}}\bigg\}=\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}\subset\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p}),

(2.13)

where the last equality is obvious because of equation (
2.10
). Hence, the functor
\mathcal{M}
is valued in the subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt{CarMan}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man
of Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
(
p,q_{1},\dots,q_{N}\in\mathbb{N}
) and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms whose coordinate pullbacks are global linear functions of the source manifold that have the appropriate degree:

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarMan.

The inverse “vectorification functor”
\mathcal{V}
of this “manifoldification functor”
\mathcal{M}
is readily defined: to a Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
we associate the Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
, and to a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism we associate the degree 0 linear map that is defined by the transpose of the block diagonal matrix coming from the morphism’s linear pullbacks. It is obvious that
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}\circ\mathcal{V}=\operatorname{id}
.

Proposition 2.5
.

We have an isomorphism of categories

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\rightleftarrows{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarMan\ \,{:}\,\mathcal{V}

(2.14)

between the full subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt{Vec}
of Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
and the subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Car}{\tt Man}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}
of Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms with linear coordinate pullbacks.

Remark 2.6
.

Let us stress that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space of linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-functions

\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}^{\operatorname{lin}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\subset\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})

is of course not an algebra. In the case
p=0
, we get

\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}}(\{\star\})=\mathchar 28675\relax^{\operatorname{lin}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}}(\{\star\})=\mathchar 28675\relax,

where
\{\star\}
denotes the 0-dimensional base manifold
{\mathbb{R}}^{0}
of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-point
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}
, where
\mathchar 28675\relax={\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28946\relax^{1}_{1},\dots
,
\mathchar 28946\relax^{q_{N}}_{N}]]
is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra that corresponds to
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}
, and where
\mathchar 28675\relax^{\operatorname{lin}}
is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space of homogeneous degree 1 polynomials in the
\mathchar 28946\relax_{1}^{1},\dots,\mathchar 28946\relax_{N}^{q_{N}}
(with vanishing term
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}^{\operatorname{lin}}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-degree zero).

We close this subsection with some observations regarding the functor of points.

The Yoneda functor of points of the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man
is the fully faithful embedding

\mathcal{Y}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}\to{\tt Fun}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt Set}\big).

In
[
13
]
, we showed that
\mathcal{Y}
remains fully faithful for appropriate restrictions of the source and target of the functor category, as well as of the
resulting
functor category. More precisely, we proved that the functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt A(N)FM\big)

(2.15)

is fully faithful. The category
\tt A(N)FM
is the category of (nuclear) Fréchet manifolds over a (nuclear) Fréchet algebra, and the functor category is the category of those functors that send a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax
to a (nuclear) Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-manifold, and of those natural transformations that have
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-smooth
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components. For any
M\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man
and any
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}\simeq\mathchar 28675\relax\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}}
, we have

M(\mathchar 28675\relax):=\mathcal{S}(M)(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\mathcal{Y}(M)(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},M\big).

On the other hand, the Yoneda functor of points of the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarVec
is the fully faithful embedding

\underline{\bullet}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\ni\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\mapsto\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}:=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\big({-},\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\in{\tt Fun}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt Set\big).

The value of this functor on
\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{m}}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}\simeq\mathchar 28675\relax
, is the
subset

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\big(\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{m}},\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarMan}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)

\displaystyle\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\mathcal{S}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(\{\star\})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\otimes\mathbf{R}e^{i}_{k}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\;\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\!\otimes\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathbf{R}e^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathchar 28675\relax\otimes\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)_{0}=\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\tt Mod.

(2.16)

More precisely,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\big(\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{m}},\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(\{\star\})=\big(\mathchar 28675\relax^{\operatorname{lin}}\otimes\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)_{0}\in\tt Set.

(2.17)

Remark that, if we denote the coordinates of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
compactly by
(u^{\mathfrak{a}})
, the bijection in equation (
2.17
) sends a degree 0 linear map
\mathbf{L}
to the linear pullbacks
L^{*}(u^{\mathfrak{a}})
of the corresponding
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
L=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})
.

Remark 2.7
.

If we restrict the functor
\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}

\big(
resp., the functor
\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\big)
from
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarMan}^{\operatorname{op}}
(resp., from
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Pts^{\operatorname{op}}
) to the joint subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarPts^{\operatorname{op}}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-points and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms with linear coordinate pullbacks, the restricted Hom functor
\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is actually a subfunctor of the restricted tensor product functor
\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)
. This observation clarifies the relationship between the fully faithful “functor of points”
\mathcal{F}(\bullet)(-)=\bullet(-)
of the full subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
and its standard fully faithful Yoneda functor of points
\underline{\bullet}(-)
.

Indeed, we observed already that the values of the Hom functor on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-points are subsets of the values of the tensor product functor. Further, on morphisms, the values of
\operatorname{Hom}\big({-},\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)
are restrictions of the values of
\big({-}\otimes\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)_{0}
. Indeed, if

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarPts}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{n}},{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}\big)\ni L\simeq\mathcal{V}(L)=\mathbf{L}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\big(\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{n}},\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{m}}\big),

the morphisms
\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathbf{L})
and
\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(L)
are defined on
\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
and its
supset

\,\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(\mathchar 28675\relax)
, respectively. When interpreting an element
\mathbf{K}
of the first as an element of the second, we use the identifications

\mathbf{K}\simeq K\simeq(K^{*}(u^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{\mathfrak{a}}\in\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}.

Similar identifications are of course required when
\mathchar 28675\relax
is replaced by
\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}
. We thus get

\underline{\mathbf{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathbf{L})(\mathbf{K})=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\big(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(\mathbf{K})=\mathbf{K}\circ\mathbf{L}\simeq K\circ L\simeq\big(L^{*}(K^{*}(u^{\mathfrak{a}}))\big)_{\mathfrak{a}}.

On the other hand, we have

\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(L^{*})\big((K^{*}(u^{\mathfrak{a}}))_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)=\big(L^{*}(K^{*}(u^{\mathfrak{a}}))\big)_{\mathfrak{a}},

since

\big(L^{*}\otimes\mathbbmss{1}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}\big)_{0}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}L^{*},

when read through the isomorphism

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\;\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\!\otimes\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathbf{R}\,e^{i}_{k}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}.

This completes the proof of the subfunctor-statement.

2.3
Finite dimensional
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-graded vector spaces and linear
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-manifolds

In this subsection, we extend equivalence (
2.14
) in a coordinate-free way.

2.3.1
Finite dimensional
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-graded vector spaces

We focus on the full subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
of finite dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector spaces, i.e., of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces
V
of finite dimension

p|\underline{q}\qquad\big(p\in\mathbb{N},\,\underline{q}=(q_{1},\dots,q_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{\times N}\big).

Clearly

{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt FinVec

is a full subcategory.

Above, we already used the canonical basis of
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
, i.e., the basis

e^{i}_{k}=\,^{t}(0\dots 0;\dots;0\dots 1\dots 0;\dots;0\dots 0),

where
1
sits in position
k
of block
i
. If

(b^{i}_{k})_{i,k}\qquad(i\in I=\{0,\dots,N\},\,k\in K_{i}=\{1,\dots,q_{i}\},\,\deg(b^{i}_{k})=\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n})

is a basis of
V
, the degree respecting linear map

\displaystyle b\colon\ V\ni v=\sum_{i,k}v^{k}_{i}b^{i}_{k}\mapsto\sum_{i,k}v^{k}_{i}e^{i}_{k}=\,^{t}\big(v_{0}^{1},\dots,v_{N}^{q_{N}}\big)=:v^{I}\in\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}

maps a basis to a basis and is thus an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces.

We already discussed extensively the functor of points
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}(\bullet)(-)=\bullet(-)
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
. Since
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt FinVec
is a full subcategory of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
, the functor
\mathcal{F}
remains fully faithful when restricted to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt FinVec
:

Proposition 2.8
.

The functor of points
\mathcal{F}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)
of the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
is fully faithful.

Remark 2.9
.

Later on, we consider linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds and denote them sometimes using the same letter
V
as for
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces. We often disambiguate the concept considered by writing
\mathbf{V}
in the vector space case.

2.3.2
Linear
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-manifolds

In this subsection, we investigate the category of linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds, linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-functions of its objects, as well as its functor of points.

Linear
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-manifolds and their morphisms.
A
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold of dimension
p|\underline{q}
is a locally
{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}_{2}
-ringed space
M:=\big(|M|,\mathcal{O}_{M}\big)
that is locally isomorphic to
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
.

Definition 2.10
.

A
linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
of dimension
p|\underline{q}
is a locally
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-ringed space
\textsf{L}=\big(|\textsf{L}|,\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}\big)
that is globally isomorphic to
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
, i.e., it is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold such that there exists a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphism

\textnormal{h}\colon\ \textsf{L}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}.

The diffeomorphism
h
is referred to as a
linear coordinate map
or a
linear one-chart-atlas
.

We now mimic classical differential geometry and say that two linear one-chart-atlases are linearly compatible, if their union is a “linear two-chart-atlas”. In other words:

Definition 2.11
.

Two linear coordinate maps
\textnormal{h}_{1},\textnormal{h}_{2}\colon\textsf{L}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
are said to be
linearly compatible
, if the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms

h_{2}\circ h_{1}^{-1},\,\textnormal{h}_{1}\circ\textnormal{h}^{-1}_{2}\colon\ {\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}

have linear coordinate pullbacks, i.e., if they are
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarMan
-morphisms.

Linear compatibility is an equivalence relation on linear one-chart-atlases. There is a
1:1
correspondence between equivalence classes of linear one-chart-atlases and maximal linear atlases, i.e., the unions of all linear one-chart-atlases of an equivalence class. For simplicity, we refer to a maximal linear atlas as a
linear atlas
.

Just as a classical smooth manifold is a set that admits an atlas, or, better, a set endowed with an equivalence class of atlases, a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold is a locally
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-ringed space
L
equipped with a linear atlas
(\textsf{L},\textnormal{h}_{\mathchar 28939\relax})_{\mathchar 28939\relax}
.

We continue working in analogy with differential geometry and define a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism between linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds as a locally
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-ringed space morphism, or, equivalently, a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism, with linear coordinate form:

Definition 2.12
.

Let
L
and
\textsf{L}^{\prime}
be two linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds of dimension
p|\underline{q}
and
r|\underline{s}
, respectively. A
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\textsf{L}\to\textsf{L}^{\prime}
is
linear
, if there exist linear coordinate maps

\textnormal{h}\colon\ \textsf{L}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\textnormal{k}\colon\ \textsf{L}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}

in the linear atlases of
L
and
\textsf{L}^{\prime}
, such that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism

\textnormal{k}\circ\phi\circ\textnormal{h}^{-1}\colon\ \mathbb{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{r|\mathbf{s}}

has linear coordinate pullbacks.

It follows that any linear coordinate map
\rm h
of the linear atlas of a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
\sf L
,
is
a
linear

{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism between the linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
\sf L
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
. This justifies the name “linear coordinate map”. Further, the inverse
{\rm h}^{-1}
of
\rm h
is a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism.

Proposition 2.13
.

If
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\sf L\to\sf L^{\prime}
is a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism, then, for any linear coordinate maps
(\sf L,\textnormal{h}^{\prime})
and
(\sf L^{\prime},\textnormal{k}^{\prime})
of the linear atlases of
\sf L
and
\sf L^{\prime}
, respectively, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\textnormal{k}^{\prime}\circ\mathchar 28958\relax\circ\textnormal{h}^{\prime-1}
has linear coordinate pullbacks.

Proof.

We use the notations of Definition
2.12
and Proposition
2.13
. Since

\textnormal{k}^{\prime}\circ\mathchar 28958\relax\circ\textnormal{h}^{\prime-1}=\big(\textnormal{k}^{\prime}\circ\textnormal{k}^{-1}\big)\circ\big(\textnormal{k}\circ\mathchar 28958\relax\circ\textnormal{h}^{-1}\big)\circ\big(\textnormal{h}\circ\textnormal{h}^{\prime-1}\big)

and each parenthesis of the
RHS
has linear pullbacks, their composite has linear pullbacks as well.
∎

Proposition 2.14
.

Linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds and linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms form a subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{LinMan}}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man
of the category of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds. Further, Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms with linear coordinate pullbacks form a full subcategory
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarMan}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
.

Proof.

If
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\textsf{L}\to\textsf{L}^{\prime}
and
\psi\colon\textsf{L}^{\prime}\to\textsf{L}^{\prime\prime}
are linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms, the composite
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism is linear as well. Indeed, if
\textnormal{k}\circ\mathchar 28958\relax\circ\textnormal{h}^{-1}
and
\textnormal{q}\circ\psi\circ\textnormal{p}^{-1}
have linear pullbacks, then
\textnormal{q}\circ\psi\circ\textnormal{k}^{-1}
has linear pullbacks and so has
\textnormal{q}\circ(\psi\circ\mathchar 28958\relax)\circ\textnormal{h}^{-1}
. Further, for any linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
L
, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-identity map
\operatorname{id}_{\textsf{L}}
is linear, as for any linear coordinate map
h
, we have
\textnormal{h}\circ\operatorname{id}_{\textsf{L}}\circ\textnormal{h}^{-1}=\operatorname{id}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}
. The second statement is obvious.∎

Sheaf of linear
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-functions.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
\textsf{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan
be of dimension
p|\underline{q}
and let
|U|\subset|\textsf{L}|
be open. A
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-function
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)
is a
linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-function
, if there exists a linear coordinate map
\textnormal{h}\colon\textsf{L}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
, such that

(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}(f)\in\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}\big(|\textnormal{h}|(|U|)\big).

We denote the subset of all linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-functions of
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)
by
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|U|)
.

The subset
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}^{\operatorname{lin}}\big(|\textnormal{h}|(|U|)\big)
is defined in the obvious way. If
f\in\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|U|)
, then for any chart
(\textsf{L},\textnormal{h}^{\prime})
of the linear atlas of
L
, we have

(\textnormal{h}^{\prime*})^{-1}(f)\in\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}\big(|\textnormal{h}^{\prime}|(|U|)\big).

This follows from the equation

(\textnormal{h}^{\prime*})^{-1}(f)=\big(\textnormal{h}\circ\textnormal{h}^{\prime-1}\big)^{*}\big((\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}(f)\big)

and the compatibility of the two charts.

As
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|U|)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)
is visibly closed for linear combinations, it is a vector subspace of
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)
. Hence, the intersection

\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|U|):=\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|U|)\cap\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(|U|)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)

is also a vector subspace. We thus get vector subspaces
\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(|U|)\subset\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)
, so their direct sum over
i
is a vector subspace as well. Since any
f\in\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)
reads uniquely as

f=\sum_{i=0}^{N}f_{i}\qquad\big(f_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(|U|)\big),

we get

(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}f_{0}+\sum_{j}(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}f_{j}=(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}f=\sum_{\ell}r_{\ell}\,x^{\ell}+\sum_{j}\sum_{\ell}r^{j}_{\ell}\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\ell}_{j}.

As
(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}
is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-degree preserving, we find that
f_{i}\in\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(|U|)
, so that

\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)=\bigoplus_{i}\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(|U|)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec.

Remark 2.16
.

Observe that:

(i)

For any open subset
|U|\subset|\textsf{L}|
and any linear coordinate map
\textnormal{h}\colon\textsf{L}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
, the map

\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ \mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}(|h|(|U|))\to\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)

is an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces of dimension
p|\underline{q}
.

(ii)

The restriction maps and the gluing property of
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}
endow
\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}
with a sheaf of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces structure.

(iii)

A
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\textsf{L}\to\textsf{L}^{\prime}
between linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds is itself linear, if and only if
\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}
is a degree respecting linear map

\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}\colon\ \mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}^{\prime}}(|\textsf{L}^{\prime}|)\to\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|\textsf{L}|).

It is straightforward to check the first two statements. For the third one, let
L
(resp.,
\textsf{L}^{\prime}
) be of dimension
p|\underline{q}
(resp.,
r|\underline{s}
) and denote the coordinates of the corresponding Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold by
u^{\mathfrak{a}}=\big(x^{a},\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}\big)

\big(
resp.,
v^{\mathfrak{b}}=\big(y^{b},\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}\big)\big)
. The morphism
\mathchar 28958\relax
is linear, if and only if there exist linear coordinate maps
(\textsf{L},\textnormal{h})
and
(\textsf{L}^{\prime},\textnormal{k})
, such that
\textnormal{k}\circ\mathchar 28958\relax\circ\,\textnormal{h}^{-1}
has linear coordinate pullbacks, i.e., such that

\displaystyle\big((\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}\circ\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}\circ\textnormal{k}^{*}\big)(v^{\mathfrak{b}})\in\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p}).

(2.18)

On the other hand, in view of the first item of the previous remark, the condition

\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}\big(\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}^{\prime}}(|\textsf{L}^{\prime}|)\big)\subset\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|\textsf{L}|)

of the third item is equivalent to asking that

\displaystyle(\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}\circ\textnormal{k}^{*})\bigg(\sum_{\mathfrak{b}}r_{\mathfrak{b}}v^{\mathfrak{b}}\bigg)\in\textnormal{h}^{*}\big(\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\big).

(2.19)

The conditions (
2.18
) and (
2.19
) are visibly equivalent.

Functor of points of
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}}
.
We start with the following

Proposition 2.17
.

For any linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
\sf{L}

(
of dimension
p|\underline{q})
and any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}
, the set

{\sf{L}}(\mathchar 28675\relax):=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},{\sf L}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Alg}(\mathcal{O}_{\sf L}(|{\sf L}|),\mathchar 28675\relax)

of
\mathchar 28675\relax
-points of
\,\sf L
admits a unique Fréchet
\Lambda_{0}
-module structure, such that, for any chart
{\rm h}\colon{\sf L}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
of the linear atlas of
\,\sf{L}
, the induced map

{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon\ {\sf{L}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\ni{\rm x}^{*}\mapsto{\rm x}^{*}\circ{\rm h}^{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)

is a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module isomorphism.

The definition of the category
\tt FAMod
of Fréchet modules over Fréchet algebras can be found in Appendix
A
. In the preceding proposition, it is implicit that the (unital) Fréchet algebra morphism that is associated to
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is
\operatorname{id}_{\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}}
.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\textnormal{{GrAlg}}
. In view of the fundamental theorem of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms, there is a
1:1
correspondence between the
\mathchar 28675\relax
-points
\rm{x}^{*}
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
and the
(p+|\underline{q}|)
-tuples

\mathrm{x}^{*}\simeq\big({x}^{a}_{\Lambda},\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}_{\Lambda}\big)\in{\Lambda}_{0}^{\times p}\times\Lambda_{\gamma_{1}}^{\times q_{1}}\times\dots\times\Lambda_{\gamma_{N}}^{\times q_{N}}

(we used this correspondence already in equation (
2.16
)). Indeed, the algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax
is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative nuclear Fréchet
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebra of global
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-functions of some
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}
(in particular, the degree zero term
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
of
\mathchar 28675\relax
is a commutative nuclear Fréchet algebra). Hence, all its homogeneous subspaces
\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
(
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
,
\mathchar 28941\relax_{0}=0
) are nuclear Fréchet vector spaces. Since any product (resp., any countable product) of nuclear (resp., Fréchet) spaces is nuclear (resp., Fréchet), the set
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
of
\mathchar 28675\relax
-points of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is a nuclear Fréchet space. The latter statements can be found in
[
14
]
. The Fréchet
\Lambda_{0}
-module structure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
is then defined by

\displaystyle\triangleleft\colon\ \Lambda_{0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\Lambda)\ni(\mathrm{a},\mathrm{x}^{*})\mapsto{\rm a}\triangleleft{\rm x}^{*}:=\big(\mathrm{a}\cdot x_{\Lambda}^{a},\,\mathrm{a}\cdot\mathchar 28952\relax_{\Lambda}^{A}\big)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\Lambda).

(2.20)

Since this action (which is compatible with addition in
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
and addition in
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
) is defined using the continuous associative multiplication
\cdot\colon\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\times\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}\to\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}+\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
of the Fréchet algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax
, it is (jointly) continuous.

We now define the
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
. Observe first that, for any chart map
{\rm h}\colon{\sf L}\rightleftarrows{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\ {:}\,{\rm h}^{-1}
of the linear atlas of
\sf L
, the induced maps
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\rightleftarrows{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\ {:}\,\big({\rm h}^{-1}\big)_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
are inverse maps:
\big({\rm h}^{-1}\big)_{\mathchar 28675\relax}=({\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax})^{-1}=:{\rm h}^{-1}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
. For
K\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
,
k\in\{1,\dots,K\}
,
{\rm a}^{k}\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
, and
{\rm y}_{k}^{*}\in{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
, we set

\sum_{k}{\rm a}^{k}\star{\rm y}_{k}^{*}:={\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{k}{\rm a}^{k}\triangleleft{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}({\rm y}_{k}^{*})\bigg)\in{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax).

This defines a
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
that makes
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
a
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module isomorphism. The
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structures
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)_{\rm h}
and
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)_{\rm k}
that are implemented by
\rm h
and another chart
\rm k
of the linear atlas, respectively, are related by the
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module isomorphism

{\rm k}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}\circ{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon\ {\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)_{\rm h}\to{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)_{\rm k}.

Hence, the
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
is well-defined.

In order to get a Fréchet structure on the real vector space
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
that we just defined, we need a countable and separating family of seminorms
(p_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, such that any sequence in
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
that is Cauchy for every
p_{n}
, converges for every
p_{n}
to a fixed vector (i.e., a vector that does not depend on
n
). We define this family (of course) by transferring to
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
the analogous family
(\mathchar 28954\relax_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of the Fréchet vector space
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
(see
[
14
, Theorem 14]
). In other words, for each
{\rm y}^{*}\in{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
, we set

p_{n}({\rm y}^{*}):=\mathchar 28954\relax_{n}({\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}({\rm y}^{*}))\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}.

It is straightforwardly checked that
(p_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a countable family of seminorms that has the required properties. Moreover, the vector space isomorphism
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is an isomorphism of Fréchet vector spaces, i.e., a continuous linear map with a continuous inverse. We show that
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is continuous for the seminorm topologies implemented by the
p_{n}
and the
\mathchar 28954\relax_{n}
, i.e., that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
C>0
, such that

\mathchar 28954\relax_{n}({\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}({\rm y}^{*}))\leq C\,p_{m}({\rm y}^{*}),

for all
{\rm y}^{*}\in{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
. This requirement is of course satisfied. Hence, the composite
{\rm k}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}\circ\,{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
of isomorphisms of Fréchet spaces is an isomorphism of Fréchet spaces, so that the Fréchet space structure on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
is well-defined.

The
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure and the Fréchet vector space structure on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
combine into a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure, if they are compatible, i.e., if the
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-action

\displaystyle\star\colon\ \mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\times{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\ni({\rm a},{\rm y}^{*})\mapsto{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}({\rm a}\triangleleft{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}({\rm y}^{*}))\in{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)

(2.21)

is continuous. The condition is obviously satisfied as this action is the composite of the continuous maps
\operatorname{id}\times{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
,
\triangleleft
and
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}
. Further, the map
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is clearly a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module isomorphism, for any
\rm h
in the linear atlas of
\sf L
.

There is obviously no other Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
with that property. Indeed, if there were, it would be isomorphic to the Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
, hence isomorphic to the Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure that we just constructed. ∎

In the following, we denote the
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-action
\star
on
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
by simple juxtaposition, i.e., we write
{\rm a}{\rm y^{*}}
instead of
{\rm a}\star{\rm y}^{*}
.

To proceed, we need some preparation.

Let
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
be the category of functors
F
, whose values
F(\mathchar 28675\relax)
are Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-modules, and of natural transformations
\mathchar 28940\relax
, whose
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components
\mathchar 28940\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
are continuous
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear maps. We already used above the category
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AFM}\big)
of functors, whose values are Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-manifolds, and of natural transformations, whose components are
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-smooth maps.

Proposition 2.18
.

The category
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
is a subcategory of the category
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AFM}\big)
.

Proof.

Observe first that composition of natural transformations (resp., identities of functors) is (resp., are) induced by composition (resp., identities) in the target category of the functors considered, which is (resp., are) in both target categories the standard set-theoretical composition (resp., identities). Hence, composition and identities are the same in both functor categories. However, we still have to show that objects (resp., morphisms) of the first functor category are objects (resp., morphisms) of the second.

Let
F
be a functor with target
\tt FAMod
. Since a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module (i.e., a Fréchet vector space with a (compatible) continuous
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-action) is clearly a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-manifold, the functor
F
sends
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebras
\mathchar 28675\relax
to Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-manifolds
F(\mathchar 28675\relax)
. Let now
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}
be a morphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebras. As
F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\colon F(\mathchar 28675\relax)\to F(\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime})
is a morphism between Fréchet modules over the Fréchet algebras
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
and
\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}_{0}
, respectively, it is continuous and it has an associated continuous (unital,
{\mathbb{R}}
-) algebra morphism
\psi\colon\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}_{0}
, such that

\displaystyle F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm a}{\rm v}+{\rm a}^{\prime}{\rm v}^{\prime})=\psi({\rm a})F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm v})+\psi({\rm a}^{\prime})F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm v}^{\prime}),

(2.22)

for all
{\rm a,a^{\prime}}\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
and all
{\rm v,v^{\prime}}\in F(\mathchar 28675\relax)
. We must show that
F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is a morphism between Fréchet manifolds over
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
and
\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}_{0}
, respectively, i.e., we must show that
F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is smooth and has first order derivatives that are linear in the sense of (
2.22
) (see
[
13
]
). Since, for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have
\psi(t)=t\psi(1)=t
, it follows from (
2.22
) that

{\rm d}_{\rm x}F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm v}):=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{1}{t}(F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm x}+t{\rm v})-F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm x}))=F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm v})

and

{\rm d}^{k+1}_{\rm x}F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm v}_{1},\dots,{\rm v}_{k+1})=0,

for any
{\rm x},{\rm v},{\rm v}_{1},\dots,{\rm v}_{k+1}\in F(\mathchar 28675\relax)
and any
k\geq 1
. Hence, all derivatives exist everywhere and are (jointly) continuous. This implies that
F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
has the required properties, so that
F
is a functor with target
\tt AFM
.

As for morphisms, let
\mathchar 28945\relax\colon F\to G
be a natural transformation between functors valued in
\tt FAMod
. Its
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components
\mathchar 28945\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon F(\mathchar 28675\relax)\to G(\mathchar 28675\relax)
are continuous and
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear maps. Repeating the proof given in the preceding paragraph for
F(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
, we obtain that
\mathchar 28945\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-smooth, i.e., is smooth and has
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear first order derivatives. Therefore, the morphism
\mathchar 28945\relax
of the functor category with target
\tt FAMod
is a morphism of the functor category with target
\tt AFM
.
∎

Since

{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)\subset{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AFM}\big)

are subcategories, we expect that:

Proposition 2.19
.

The functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt AFM\big)

(
see equation (
2.15
)
)
restricts to a functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt FAMod\big).

Proof.

We have to explain why
\mathcal{S}
sends linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds and linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms to objects and morphisms, respectively, of the target subcategory.

Let
{\sf L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
. The functor
\mathcal{S}({\sf L})
is an object of the functor category with target
\tt AFM
. Since composition and identities are the same in both target categories, it suffices to show that, for any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-Grassmann algebra
\mathchar 28675\relax
, the value
\mathcal{S}({\sf L})(\mathchar 28675\relax)={\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
is a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module and that, for any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}
, the morphism

{\sf L}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\colon\ {\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\ni{\rm y}^{*}\mapsto\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\circ{\rm y}^{*}\in{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime})

is a morphism of the category
\tt FAMod
. The first of the preceding conditions holds in view of Proposition
2.17
. We start proving the second condition for
{\sf L}={\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
. Since
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is a morphism of
\tt AFM
, it is smooth, hence, continuous. Further, omitting the summation symbols and using our standard notation, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\big({\rm a}^{k}\triangleleft{\rm x}_{k}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\big({\rm a}^{k}\cdot x_{\mathchar 28675\relax,k}^{a},{\rm a}^{k}\cdot\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax,k}^{A}\big)=\big(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big({\rm a}^{k}\big)\cdot\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}(x_{\mathchar 28675\relax,k}^{a}),\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big({\rm a}^{k}\big)\cdot\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big(\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax,k}^{A}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big({\rm a}^{k}\big)\,\triangleleft\;{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm x}^{*}_{k}).

It now suffices to recall that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}
is the pullback
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}_{\star}
over the whole base manifold
\{\star\}
of a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}^{\prime}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}
, and that all pullbacks of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphisms are continuous, so that the restriction
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}_{0}
is a continuous algebra morphism. We are now able to prove that the second condition holds also for an arbitrary linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
\sf L
. Indeed, since
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}
is a morphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebras, the map
{\sf L}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\colon{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\to{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime})
is a morphism of
\tt AFM
, hence, it is continuous. Recall now that any chart
{\rm h}\colon{\sf L}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism, so that
\mathcal{S}({\rm h})\colon{\sf L}(-)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(-)
is a natural transformation
{\rm h}_{-}
with
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components
{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon{\sf L}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
that are Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
