-module isomorphisms in view of Proposition
2.17
. Naturality of
{\rm h}_{-}
implies that

{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}}\circ{\sf L}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})={\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\circ{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax},

and, due to invertibility, that

{\sf L}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})={\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\circ{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}.

Definition (
2.21
) yields

{\sf L}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\big({\rm a}^{k}\star{\rm y}^{*}_{k}\big)=\big({\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\circ{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\big)\big({\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}\big({\rm a}^{k}\triangleleft{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}({\rm y}^{*}_{k})\big)\big)=\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big({\rm a}^{k}\big)\star{\sf L}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})({\rm y}^{*}_{k})

(we used our standard notation). Hence, the functor
\mathcal{S}({\sf L})
is an object of the functor category with target
\tt FAMod
.

As for morphisms, we consider a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism

\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\ {\sf L}\to{\sf L}^{\prime}

and will prove that
\mathcal{S}(\mathchar 28958\relax)
, which is a natural transformation
\mathchar 28958\relax_{-}
of the functor category with target
\tt AFM
, i.e., a natural transformation with
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-smooth
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components
\mathchar 28958\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
, has actually continuous (but this results from
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-smoothness)
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear components.

Let
p|\underline{q}
(resp.,
r|\underline{s}
) be the dimension of
\sf L
(resp., of
\sf L^{\prime}
). We first discuss the case of a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism

\Phi\colon\ {\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}

between the corresponding Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds with canonical coordinates
\big(x^{a},\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}\big)
and
\big(y^{b},\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}\big)
, respectively. We know from
[
13
]
that, if the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism (resp., the linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism)
\Phi
reads

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}\big(y^{b}\big)=\sum_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 0}\Phi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}^{b}(x)\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\qquad\bigg(\text{resp.,}\;=\sum_{a}{\mathbf{L}}^{b}_{a}x^{a}\bigg),

(2.23a)

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}\big(\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}\big)=\sum_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|>0}\Phi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}^{B}(x)\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\qquad\bigg(\text{resp.,}\;=\sum_{A}{\mathbf{L}}^{B}_{A}\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}\bigg)

(2.23b)

(where the right-hand sides have the appropriate degree and where the coefficients
\mathbf{L}^{\ast}_{\ast}
are real numbers), then the
\mathchar 28675\relax
-component
\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
associates to the
\mathchar 28675\relax
-point
{\rm x}^{*}\simeq\big(x^{a}_{\Lambda};\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}_{\Lambda}\big)=\big(x^{a}_{||},\mathring{x}_{\Lambda}^{a};\mathchar 28952\relax^{A}_{\Lambda}\big)
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
, the
\mathchar 28675\relax
-point
{\rm x}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}\simeq\big(y_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{b};\mathchar 28945\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{B}\big)
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
that is given by

\displaystyle y^{b}_{\Lambda}=\sum_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 0}\sum_{|\mathchar 28940\relax|\geq 0}\frac{1}{\mathchar 28940\relax!}\,\big(\partial_{x}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\Phi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}^{b}\big)\big(x_{||}\big)\,\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\qquad\bigg(\text{resp.,}\;=\sum_{a}{\mathbf{L}}^{b}_{a}x_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{a}\bigg),

(2.24a)

\displaystyle\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}_{\Lambda}=\sum_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|>0}\sum_{|\mathchar 28940\relax|\geq 0}\frac{1}{\mathchar 28940\relax!}\,\big(\partial_{x}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\Phi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}^{B}\big)\big(x_{||}\big)\,\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\qquad\bigg(\text{resp.,}\;=\sum_{A}{\mathbf{L}}^{B}_{A}\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{A}\bigg).

(2.24b)

Here, we used the obvious decomposition
\Lambda={\mathbb{R}}\times\mathring{\Lambda}
and wrote
x^{a}_{\Lambda}=(x^{a}_{||},\mathring{x}^{a}_{\Lambda})
. The particular linear versions of equations (
2.24a
) and (
2.24b
) (in parentheses), show that the component
\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear, as needed.

In the general case of a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\phi\colon{\sf L}\to{\sf L}^{\prime}
, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\Phi:={\rm k}\circ\phi\circ{\rm h}^{-1}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
has linear coordinate pullbacks
\Phi^{*}\big(y^{b}\big)
and
\Phi^{*}\big(\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}\big)
(and is thus a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism), for any charts
\rm h
and
\rm k
of
\sf L
and
{\sf L}^{\prime}
, respectively. Since
\mathchar 28958\relax={\rm k}^{-1}\circ\Phi\circ{\rm h}
, we have
\mathchar 28958\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}={\rm k}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\circ{\rm h}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
and, in view of Proposition
2.17
and the result of the preceding paragraph, all three factors of the
RHS
are
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear.

Finally, the natural transformation
\mathcal{S}(\mathchar 28958\relax)
is a natural transformation of the functor category with target
\tt FAMod
.∎

Theorem 2.20
.

The functor of points

\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt FAMod\big)

of the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan
is fully faithful.

Proof.

We need to prove that the map

\mathcal{S}_{\sf L,L^{\prime}}\colon\ \operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan}({\sf L,L^{\prime}})\ni\phi\mapsto\mathchar 28958\relax_{-}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\tt Fun}_{0}({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt FAMod)}({\sf L}(-),{\sf L^{\prime}}(-))

is bijective, for all linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
\sf L,\ L^{\prime}
.

Since
\mathcal{S}
is the restriction of the fully faithful functor
\mathcal{S}\colon{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AFM}\big)
, the map
\mathcal{S}_{\sf L,L^{\prime}}
is injective.

To prove that
\mathcal{S}_{\sf L,L^{\prime}}
is also surjective, it actually suffices to show that the property holds for Cartesian
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds. Indeed, in this case, if
\mathchar 28945\relax\colon{\sf L}(-)\to{\sf L}^{\prime}(-)
is a natural transformation of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt FAMod\big)
, then
{\rm k}_{-}\circ\mathchar 28945\relax\circ{\rm h}^{-1}_{-}
is a natural transformation in the same category from
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(-)
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}(-)
, and this transformation is implemented by a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
. It follows that

\mathchar 28945\relax={\rm k}_{-}^{-1}\circ\mathchar 28967\relax_{-}\circ{\rm h}_{-}=({\rm k}^{-1}\circ\mathchar 28967\relax\circ{\rm h})_{-},

where the latter composite is a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism.

Let now
H\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(-)\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}(-)
be a natural transformation of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt FAMod\big)
, hence, a natural transformation of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt AFM\big)
. We know from
[
13
]
that
H
is implemented by a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\Phi\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
, but we still have to prove that this morphism is linear. It follows from equations (
2.24a
) and (
2.24b
) that
H_{\mathchar 28675\relax}=\Phi_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is given by

\displaystyle y^{b}_{\Lambda}=\sum_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|\geq 0}\sum_{|\mathchar 28940\relax|\geq 0}F^{b}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(x_{||}\big)\,\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28939\relax},

(2.25a)

\displaystyle\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}_{\Lambda}=\sum_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|>0}\sum_{|\mathchar 28940\relax|\geq 0}F^{B}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(x_{||}\big)\,\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28939\relax},

(2.25b)

where we set

\displaystyle F^{\ast}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}(x):=\frac{1}{\mathchar 28940\relax!}\partial_{x}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\Phi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}^{\ast}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})

(2.26)

(the
\Phi^{*}_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})
are the coefficients of the coordinate pullbacks by
\Phi
, see equations (
2.23a
) and (
2.23b
)), and where the
RHS
-s have of course the same
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-degree as the corresponding coordinates of
{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
. Since
H_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear, we have

\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\sum_{\mathchar 28940\relax}F^{\ast}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(r\,x_{||}\big)\,r^{|\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|}\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28939\relax}=r\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\sum_{\mathchar 28940\relax}F^{\ast}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(x_{||}\big)\,\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28940\relax}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{\mathchar 28939\relax},

i.e.,

r^{|\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|}F^{\ast}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(r\,x_{||}\big)=r\,F^{\ast}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(x_{||}\big),

for any
r\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\subset\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
, any
\mathchar 28939\relax
,
\mathchar 28940\relax
and for any
x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
. When deriving with respect to
r
, we obtain

r^{|\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|-1}\bigg((|\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|)F^{\ast}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big(rx_{||}\big)+r\sum_{a=1}^{p}x_{||}^{a}\big(\partial_{x^{a}_{||}}F^{*}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}\big)\big(rx_{||}\big)\bigg)=F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}\big(x_{||}\big),

so that setting
r=1
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{p}x_{||}^{a}\partial_{x^{a}_{||}}F^{*}_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}=(1-n)F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}\big(x_{||}\big)\qquad(n:=|\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|\in\mathbb{N}),

(2.27)

again for all
\mathchar 28939\relax
,
\mathchar 28940\relax
and all
x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
.

Recall now that Euler’s homogeneous function theorem states that, if
F\in C^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{p}\setminus\{0\})
, then, for any
\mathchar 28951\relax\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\sum_{a=1}^{p}x^{a}\partial_{x^{a}}F=\mathchar 28951\relax F(x),\qquad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\setminus\{0\}

is equivalent to

F(rx)=r^{\mathchar 28951\relax}F(x),\qquad\forall r>0,\quad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\setminus\{0\}.

In view of (
2.27
), we thus get

\displaystyle F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}\big(rx_{||}\big)=r^{1-n}F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}\big(x_{||}\big),\qquad\forall r>0,\quad\forall x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p},

(2.28)

where we could extend the equality from
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\setminus\{0\}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
due to continuity. If
r
tends to
0^{+}
, the limit of the
LHS
is
F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}(0)\in{\mathbb{R}}
and, for
n=0
(resp.,
n=1
; resp.,
n\geq 2
), the limit of the
RHS
is
0

\big(
resp.,
F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}\big(x_{||}\big)
; resp.,
+\infty\cdot F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{\ast}\big(x_{||}\big)\big)
.

In the case
n\geq 2
, we conclude that

\displaystyle F_{\mathchar 28939\relax\mathchar 28940\relax}^{*}\big(x_{||}\big)=0,\qquad\forall x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p},\quad\forall\mathchar 28939\relax,\mathchar 28940\relax\colon\ |\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|\geq 2.

(2.29)

For
n=0
, we get

F^{*}_{00}(0)=0.

Observe that
\mathchar 28939\relax=\mathchar 28940\relax=0
is only possible in equation (
2.25a
). Differentiating (
2.28
), in the case
n=0
, with respect to any component
x_{||}^{a}
of
x_{||}
and simplifying by
r
, we obtain

\big(\partial_{x^{a}_{||}}F_{00}^{b}\big)\big(rx_{||}\big)=\partial_{x_{||}^{a}}F^{b}_{00}\big(x_{||}\big),

and taking the limit
r\to 0^{+}
, we get

\partial_{x_{||}^{a}}F^{b}_{00}\big(x_{||}\big)=\partial_{x_{||}^{a}}F^{b}_{00}(0)=:\mathbf{L}^{b}_{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Integration yields

\displaystyle F^{b}_{00}\big(x_{||}\big)=\sum_{a}\mathbf{L}^{b}_{a}x_{||}^{a},\qquad\forall x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p},\quad\forall b,

(2.30)

as
F_{00}^{b}(0)=0
.

In the remaining case
n=|\mathchar 28939\relax|+|\mathchar 28940\relax|=1
, we have necessarily
\mathchar 28939\relax=0
and
\mathchar 28940\relax=e_{a}
, or
\mathchar 28939\relax=e_{A}
and
\mathchar 28940\relax=0
(the
e_{*}
are of course the vectors of the canonical basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{|\underline{q}|}
, respectively). For
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-degree reasons, the first (resp., second) possibility is incompatible with equation (
2.25b
) (resp., equation (
2.25a
)). Hence, the only terms in (
2.25a
) that still need being investigated are the terms
(\mathchar 28939\relax,\mathchar 28940\relax)=(0,e_{a})
. It follows from equation (
2.28
) and its limit
r\to 0^{+}
(see above) that
F_{0\,e_{a}}^{b}\big(x_{||}\big)=\mathbf{K}^{b}_{a}
, where we set
\mathbf{K}^{b}_{a}:=F^{b}_{0\,e_{a}}(0)\in{\mathbb{R}}
. However, equations (
2.26
) and (
2.30
) imply that

\mathbf{K}^{b}_{a}=F^{b}_{0\,e_{a}}\big(x_{||}\big)=\partial_{x_{||}^{a}}F^{b}_{00}\big(x_{||}\big)=\mathbf{L}^{b}_{a},

so that

\displaystyle F_{0\,e_{a}}^{b}\big(x_{||}\big)=\mathbf{L}^{b}_{a}\in{\mathbb{R}},\qquad\forall x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p},\quad\forall a,b.

(2.31)

In equation (
2.25b
), the only terms that still need being investigated are the terms
(\mathchar 28939\relax,\mathchar 28940\relax)=(e_{A},0)
. Using again the limit
r\to 0^{+}
of equation (
2.28
), we find

\displaystyle F^{B}_{e_{A}0}\big(x_{||}\big)=\mathbf{L}^{B}_{A},\qquad\forall x_{||}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p},\quad\forall A,B,

(2.32)

where we wrote
\mathbf{L}^{B}_{A}
instead of
F^{B}_{e_{A}0}(0)
.

When combining now the results of equations (
2.29
), (
2.30
), (
2.31
), and (
2.32
), we see that equations (
2.25a
) and (
2.25b
) reduce to

\displaystyle y^{b}_{\Lambda}=\sum_{a}\mathbf{L}^{b}_{a}\,\big(x_{||}^{a}+\mathring{x}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{a}\big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}_{\Lambda}=\sum_{A}\mathbf{L}^{B}_{A}\,\mathchar 28952\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{A}\;

and that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\Phi
that induces the natural transformation
H
is defined by the coordinate pullbacks

\Phi^{*}\big(y^{b}\big)=\sum_{a}\mathbf{L}^{b}_{a}\,x^{a}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Phi^{*}\big(\mathchar 28945\relax^{B}\big)=\sum_{A}\mathbf{L}^{B}_{A}\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{A},

i.e., that
\Phi
is linear (see (
2.23a
), (
2.23b
), (
2.24a
), and (
2.24b
)).∎

2.3.3
Isomorphism between finite dimensional
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-graded vector spaces

and linear
\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-manifolds

In this subsection, we extend the isomorphism

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\rightleftharpoons{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarMan}\ \,{:}\,\mathcal{V}

of Proposition
2.5
between the full subcategories
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarMan}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
, to an isomorphism

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\rightleftharpoons{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\ \,{:}\,\mathcal{V}.

\boldsymbol{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}}
-symmetric tensor algebra.
We start with some remarks on tensor and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-symmetric tensor algebras over a (finite dimensional)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space (see
[
36
]
and
[
9
]
).

Let

V=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}V_{i}:=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}V_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}

be of dimension
p|\underline{q}
. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-symmetric tensor algebra of
V
is defined exactly as in the non-graded case, as the quotient of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded associative unital tensor algebra of
V
by the homogeneous ideal

\bar{I}=\big(v_{i}\otimes v_{j}-(-1)^{\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle}v_{j}\otimes v_{i}\colon v_{i}\in V_{i},\,v_{j}\in V_{j}\big).

More precisely, for
k\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle V^{\otimes k}=\bigoplus_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}=0}^{N}V_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes V_{i_{k}}=\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}:=\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}\bigg(\bigoplus_{\mathchar 28955\relax\in\operatorname{Perm}}V_{\mathchar 28955\relax_{i_{1}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes V_{\mathchar 28955\relax_{i_{k}}}\bigg),

where Perm is the set of all permutations of
i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}
. For instance, if
n=1
, i.e., in the standard super case, the space
V^{\otimes 3}
is the direct sum of the tensor products whose three factors have the subscripts
000
,
001
,
010
,
011
,
100
,
101
,
110
,
111
. The notation we just introduced means that we write

\displaystyle V^{\otimes 3}=V_{000}\oplus V_{001}\oplus V_{011}\oplus V_{111},

where we used the lexicographical order and where

V_{000}=V_{0}\otimes V_{0}\otimes V_{0},\qquad V_{001}=V_{0}\otimes V_{0}\otimes V_{1}\oplus V_{0}\otimes V_{1}\otimes V_{0}\oplus V_{1}\otimes V_{0}\otimes V_{0},\quad\text{et cetera}.

Further, as we are dealing with formal power series in this paper, we define the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded tensor algebra of
V
by

\overline{T}V:=\Pi_{k}V^{\otimes k},

where
\Pi_{k}
means that we consider not only finite sums of tensors of different tensor degrees, but full sequences of such tensors. The vector space structure on such sequences is obvious and the algebra structure is defined exactly as in the standard case. Indeed, for
T^{k}\in V^{\otimes k}
and
U^{\ell}\in V^{\otimes\ell}
, we have
T^{k}\otimes U^{\ell}\in V^{\otimes(k+\ell)}
and we just extend this tensor product by linearity. In other words, if

T=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}T^{k}\in\overline{T}V\qquad\text{and}\qquad U=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}U^{\ell}\in\overline{T}V,

we set

\displaystyle T\otimes U=\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell}T^{k}\otimes U^{\ell}=\sum_{m}\sum_{k+\ell=m}T^{k}\otimes U^{\ell}\in\overline{T}V.

(2.33)

It is clear that the just defined tensor multiplication endows
\overline{T}V
with a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded algebra structure. Indeed, since

V^{\otimes k}=\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{N}\!\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}\\
\sum_{j}\mathchar 28941\relax_{i_{j}}=\mathchar 28941\relax_{p}\end{subarray}}V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}=:\bigoplus_{p=0}^{N}\,\big(V^{\otimes k}\big)_{p}

is visibly a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space, the space
\overline{T}V
is itself
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded:

\overline{T}V=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{p=0}^{N}\,\big(V^{\otimes k}\big)_{p}=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{N}\Pi_{k}\,\big(V^{\otimes k}\big)_{p}=:\bigoplus_{p=0}^{N}\,\big(\overline{T}V\big)_{p}.

Now, if
T\in\big(\overline{T}V\big)_{p}
and
U\in\big(\overline{T}V\big)_{q}
, we have
T^{k}\in\big(V^{\otimes k}\big)_{p}
and
U^{\ell}\in\big(V^{\otimes\ell}\big)_{q}
, so that
T\otimes U\in\big(\overline{T}V\big)_{p+q}
(where
p+q
means
\mathchar 28941\relax_{p}+\mathchar 28941\relax_{q}
), which shows that
\overline{T}V
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded (associative unital) algebra (over
{\mathbb{R}}
), as announced.

The ideal
\bar{I}
is homogeneous with respect to the decomposition

\overline{T}V=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}},\qquad\text{i.e.,}\qquad\bar{I}=\Pi_{k(\geq 2)}\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}\big(V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}\cap\bar{I}\big).

Therefore, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-symmetric tensor algebra of
V
is given by

\displaystyle\bar{S}V

\displaystyle=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}/\big(V_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}\cap\bar{I}\big)=:\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}V_{i_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot V_{i_{k}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{N}\;\,\Pi_{k}\!\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}\\
{\sum_{j}\mathchar 28941\relax_{i_{j}}=\mathchar 28941\relax_{p}}\end{subarray}}V_{i_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot V_{i_{k}},

(2.34)

see
[
9
]
. We denote by
\odot
the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative multiplication that is induced on
\bar{S}V
by the multiplication
\otimes
of
\overline{T}V
. By definition, we have, for
[T]\in(\bar{S}V)_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
and
[U]\in(\bar{S}V)_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
(obvious notation),

[T]\odot[U]=[T\otimes U]=(-1)^{\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle}[U]\odot[T].

For instance, if
v_{i}\in V_{i}\subset(\bar{S}V)_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}
,
v_{j}\in V_{j}\subset(\bar{S}V)_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}}
and if
i\leq j
, we get

\displaystyle v_{i}\odot v_{j}=[v_{i}\otimes v_{j}]=\big[(-1)^{\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle}v_{j}\otimes v_{i}\big]=(-1)^{\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle}v_{j}\odot v_{i}\in V_{i}\odot V_{j}.

(2.35)

Notice further that, if
i<j
, the linear map

\displaystyle\iota\colon\ V_{i}\otimes V_{j}\ni T\mapsto[T]\in V_{i}\odot V_{j}\qquad\ (\iota\colon\ V_{i}\otimes V_{j}\ni v_{i}\otimes v_{j}\mapsto v_{i}\odot v_{j}\in V_{i}\odot V_{j})

(2.36)

is a vector space isomorphism. Indeed, if
[T]=0
, the representative
T
is a vector in
(V_{i}\otimes V_{j}\oplus V_{j}\otimes V_{i})\cap\bar{I}
and is therefore a finite sum of generators of
\bar{I}
:

\displaystyle(-1)^{\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle}\sum_{k}v^{k}_{j}\otimes v^{k}_{i}=\sum_{k}v^{k}_{i}\otimes v^{k}_{j}-T\in(V_{i}\otimes V_{j})\cap(V_{j}\otimes V_{i})=\{0\}.

(2.37)

It follows that the
LHS
of
equation (
2.37
) vanishes; hence, the first term of the
RHS
vanishes, due to the isomorphism
V_{i}\otimes V_{j}\simeq V_{j}\otimes V_{i}
, and thus
T
vanishes as well. In order to show that
\iota
is also surjective, consider an arbitrary vector in
V_{i}\odot V_{j}
. It reads

[T]=\bigg[\sum_{k}v_{i}^{k}\otimes v_{j}^{k}+\sum_{\ell}w_{j}^{\ell}\otimes w_{i}^{\ell}\bigg].

The image by
\iota
of

\sum_{k}v_{i}^{k}\otimes v_{j}^{k}+(-1)^{\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle}\sum_{\ell}w_{i}^{\ell}\otimes w_{j}^{\ell}\in V_{i}\otimes V_{j}

is the corresponding class. This class coincides with
[T]
, since the difference of the representatives is a vector of
\bar{I}
.

It follows that, for
n=2
for instance, we have in particular

\displaystyle V_{00}\odot V_{00}\odot V_{01}\odot V_{10}\odot V_{10}\odot V_{10}\odot V_{11}

\displaystyle\simeq\odot^{2}V_{00}\otimes V_{01}\otimes\odot^{3}V_{10}\otimes V_{11}

\displaystyle\simeq\vee^{2}V_{00}\otimes V_{01}\otimes\wedge^{3}V_{10}\otimes V_{11},

(2.38)

where
\vee
(resp.,
\wedge
) is the symmetric (resp., antisymmetric) tensor product. Moreover, if the (finite dimensional) vector space
V
has dimension
q_{0}|q_{1}
,
q_{2}
,
q_{3}
, we denote the vectors of its basis (in accordance with the notation we adopted earlier in this text) by
b^{i}_{j}
, where
i\in\{0,1,2,3\}
refers to the degrees
00
,
01
,
10
,
11
and where
j\in\{1,\dots,q_{i}\}
. The basis of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-symmetric tensor product (
2.38
) is then made of the tensors

b^{0}_{j_{1}}\vee b^{0}_{j_{2}}\otimes b^{1}_{j_{3}}\otimes b^{2}_{j_{4}}\wedge b^{2}_{j_{5}}\wedge b^{2}_{j_{6}}\otimes b^{3}_{j_{7}}

(j_{1}\leq j_{2}\;\text{and}\;j_{4}<j_{5}<j_{6})
, which can also be written

b^{0}_{j_{1}}\odot b^{0}_{j_{2}}\odot b^{1}_{j_{3}}\odot b^{2}_{j_{4}}\odot b^{2}_{j_{5}}\odot b^{2}_{j_{6}}\odot b^{3}_{j_{7}}

(j_{1}\leq j_{2}\;\text{and}\;j_{4}<j_{5}<j_{6})
(see (
2.36
)). More generally, the basis of
V_{i_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot V_{i_{k}}
(
i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}
) is made of the tensors

\displaystyle b^{i_{1}}_{j_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot b^{i_{k}}_{j_{k}}

(2.39)

(
j_{\ell}\leq j_{\ell+1}
(resp.,
<
), if
i_{\ell}=i_{\ell+1}
and
\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{i_{\ell}},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i_{\ell+1}}\rangle
even (resp., odd)). To refer to the previous condition regarding the
j
-s, we write in the following
j_{1}\lhd\cdots\lhd j_{k}
.

Observe also that

S^{k}V=\bigoplus_{i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}V_{i_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot V_{i_{k}}=S^{k}\bigoplus_{i}V_{i}=\bigg(\bigotimes_{i}SV_{i}\bigg)^{k},

as well as that, in order to define a linear map on
V_{i_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot V_{i_{k}}
(see (
2.38
)), it suffices to define a
k
-linear map on
V_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{i_{k}}
that is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative in the variables
i_{\ell}=\cdots=i_{m}
.

Manifoldification functor.
If
V
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space, its dual
V^{\vee}
is defined by

V^{\vee}:=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(V,{\mathbb{R}})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(V,{\mathbb{R}})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},{\mathbb{R}})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}(V_{i})^{\vee}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec.

More explicitly, we consider the space of
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear maps from
V
to
{\mathbb{R}}
of any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-degree. It is clear that the linear maps of degree
\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}
are the linear maps from
V_{i}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
(that vanish in any other degree). Hence,

\big(V^{\vee}\big)_{i}=(V_{i})^{\vee}=:V_{i}^{\vee}.

It follows that, if
V
is finite dimensional of dimension
p|\underline{q}
, its dual
V^{\vee}
has the same dimension. Moreover, any basis
(b^{i}_{k})_{i,k}
(
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
and
k\in\{1,\dots,q_{i}\}
, where we set
q_{0}:=p
) of
V
defines a dual basis
(\mathchar 28940\relax^{k}_{i})_{i,k}
of
V^{\vee}
.

Let now
V\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt FinVec
be of dimension
p|\underline{q}
. We set

V^{\vee}_{*}:=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{N}V^{\vee}_{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\qquad\big(\operatorname{dim}\big(V^{\vee}_{*}\big)=0|\underline{q}\big).

Proposition 2.21
.

If
V
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded vector space of dimension
p|\underline{q}
, there is a non-canonical isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative associative unital
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebras

\displaystyle\flat\colon\ \bar{S}\big(V^{\vee}_{*}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]],

where
{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]
is the global function algebra of
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}
.

Proof.

As usual, we ordered the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-degrees lexicographically, so that the
\mathchar 28952\relax_{j}^{\ell}
-s are ordered unambiguously. We have

{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]=\Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax},

where the multi-index
\mathchar 28939\relax
has components
\mathchar 28939\relax_{j}^{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}
(resp.,
\mathchar 28939\relax_{j}^{\ell}\in\{0,1\}
), if
\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{j},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle
is even (resp., odd).

On the other hand, it follows from equations (
2.34
) and (
2.39
) that, choosing a basis
\big(b^{j}_{\ell}\big)_{j,\ell}
of
V_{*}
(defined similarly as
V^{\vee}_{*}
) and denoting its dual basis by
(\mathchar 28940\relax^{\ell}_{j})_{j,\ell}
, leads to

\displaystyle\bar{S}\big(V^{\vee}_{*}\big)=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{j_{1}\leq\cdots\leq j_{k}}\bigoplus_{\ell_{1}\lhd\cdots\lhd\ell_{k}}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax_{j_{1}}^{\ell_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot\mathchar 28940\relax_{j_{k}}^{\ell_{k}}=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{|\mathchar 28939\relax|=k}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}=\Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax},

(2.40)

where
\mathchar 28939\relax_{j}^{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}
(resp.,
\mathchar 28939\relax_{j}^{\ell}\in\{0,1\}
), if
\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{j},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle
is even (resp., odd).

In view of (
2.33
) and (
2.35
), the multiplications of
{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]
and
\bar{S}\big(V^{\vee}_{*}\big)
are exactly the same, so that the two
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative algebras are canonically isomorphic, once a basis of
V_{*}
has been chosen.
∎

Remark 2.22
.

We denoted the isomorphism by
\flat
to remind us of its dependence on the basis
\big(b^{j}_{\ell}\big)_{j,\ell}
.

We are now prepared to define the linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold associated to a finite dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space. From here we denote the vector space by
\mathbf{V}
instead of
V
and reserve the notation
V
for the manifold
V:=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})
.

Hence, let
\mathbf{V}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt FinVec
be of dimension
p|\underline{q}
. The
p
-dimensional vector space
\mathbf{V}_{0}
of degree
0
is of course a smooth manifold of dimension
p
, as well as a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
V_{0}
of dimension
p|\underline{0}
. On the other hand, the algebra
\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{*}\big)
is a sheaf of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative associative unital
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebras over
\{\star\}
, i.e., it is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-ringed space with underlying topological space
\{\star\}
, and, in view of Proposition
2.21
, this space is (non-canonically) globally isomorphic to
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}=(\{\star\},{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]])
. Hence, the space
\big(\{\star\},\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{V}_{*}^{\vee}\big)\big)
is a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
V_{>}
of dimension
0|\underline{q}
. Finally, the product
V=V_{0}\times V_{>}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold of dimension
p|\underline{q}
, with base manifold
V_{0}\times\{\star\}\simeq V_{0}
and function sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{V}
that is, for any open subset
\mathchar 28682\relax\subset V_{0}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
, given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{V}(\mathchar 28682\relax)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{V_{0}\times V_{>}}(\mathchar 28682\relax\times\{\star\})=C^{\infty}_{V_{0}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathcal{O}_{V_{>}}(\{\star\})\simeq C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]

\displaystyle=C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]=\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)

(2.41)

(since
\mathchar 28682\relax
and
\{\star\}
are
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-chart domains; for more information about the problem with the function sheaf of product
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds, we refer the reader to
[
15
]
). In particular, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebras
\mathcal{O}_{V}(V_{0})
and
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})
are isomorphic (see also Definition 13 of product
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds in
[
15
]
), so that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
V
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
are diffeomorphic (given what has been said above, the diffeomorphism is implemented by the choice of a basis of
\mathbf{V}
). Finally
V\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan

\big(\operatorname{dim}V=p|\underline{q}\big)
. We define the manifoldification functor
\mathcal{M}
on objects by

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})=V.

We now define
\mathcal{M}
on morphisms. A degree zero linear map
\mathbf{L}\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{W}
between finite dimensional vector spaces (of dimensions
p|\underline{q}
and
r|\underline{s}
, respectively) is a family of linear maps
\mathbf{L}_{i}\colon\mathbf{V}_{i}\to\mathbf{W}_{i}
(
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
). We denote the transpose maps by
{}^{t}\mathbf{L}_{i}\colon\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\vee}\to\mathbf{V}_{i}^{\vee}
.

The linear map
\mathbf{L}_{0}\colon\mathbf{V}_{0}\to\mathbf{W}_{0}
is of course a smooth map
L_{0}\colon V_{0}\to W_{0}
, where
V_{0},W_{0}
are the vector spaces
\mathbf{V}_{0},\mathbf{W}_{0}
viewed as smooth manifolds. The map
L_{0}
can also be interpreted as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
L_{0}\colon V_{0}\to W_{0}
between the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
V_{0},W_{0}
(which are of dimension zero in all non-zero degrees). The base morphism of
L_{0}
is
L_{0}
itself and, for any open subset
\mathchar 28682\relax\subset W_{0}
, the pullback
(L_{0})^{*}_{\mathchar 28682\relax}
is the (unital) algebra morphism
-\circ L_{0}|_{\mathchar 28961\relax}\colon C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\to C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28961\relax)

\big(\mathchar 28961\relax:=L_{0}^{-1}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\big)
that extends the transpose
{}^{t}\mathbf{L}_{0}(-)=-\circ\mathbf{L}_{0}
.

The linear maps
{}^{t}\mathbf{L}_{j}\colon\mathbf{W}_{j}^{\vee}\to\mathbf{V}_{j}^{\vee}
(
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
) define a linear map

{\bar{S}}\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)\colon\ {\bar{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{*}^{\vee}\big)\to{\bar{S}}\big(\mathbf{V}_{*}^{\vee}\big).

Observe first that to define such a map, it suffices to define a linear map in each tensor degree
k
, hence, it suffices to define a linear map

\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)^{\odot k}_{j_{1}\dots j_{k}}\colon\ \mathbf{W}_{j_{1}}^{\vee}\odot\cdots\odot\mathbf{W}_{j_{k}}^{\vee}\to\mathbf{V}_{j_{1}}^{\vee}\odot\cdots\odot\mathbf{V}_{j_{k}}^{\vee},

for any
j_{1}\leq\dots\leq j_{k}
(
j_{a}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
). Since the
k
-linear maps

\displaystyle\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)^{\times k}_{j_{1}\dots j_{k}}\colon\ \mathbf{W}_{j_{1}}^{\vee}\times\cdots\times\mathbf{W}_{j_{k}}^{\vee}\ni\big(\mathchar 28961\relax^{1}_{j_{1}},\dots,\mathchar 28961\relax^{k}_{j_{k}}\big)\mapsto

\hphantom{\big(^t\mathbf{L}\big)^{\times k}_{j_1\ldots j_k}\colon\ }{}^{t}\mathbf{L}_{j_{1}}\big(\mathchar 28961\relax^{1}_{j_{1}}\big)\odot\cdots\odot\,^{t}\mathbf{L}_{j_{k}}\big(\mathchar 28961\relax^{k}_{j_{k}}\big)\in\mathbf{V}_{j_{1}}^{\vee}\odot\cdots\odot\mathbf{V}_{j_{k}}^{\vee}

are
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-commutative in the variables
j_{\ell}=\cdots=j_{m}
, they define the degree zero linear maps
\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)^{\odot k}_{j_{1}\dots j_{k}}

\big(
we set
\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)^{\odot\,0}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}}\big)
and thus the degree zero linear map
\bar{S}\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)
that we are looking for. In view of our definitions, the latter is a (unital)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra morphism between the global function algebras of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
W_{>}
and
V_{>}
, and it therefore defines a unique
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
L_{>}\colon V_{>}\to W_{>}
. The base morphism of
L_{>}
is the identity
c\colon\{\star\}\to\{\star\}
.

We thus get a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}):=L:=L_{0}\times L_{>}\colon\ \mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})=V=V_{0}\times V_{>}\to\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{W})=W=W_{0}\times W_{>},

(2.42)

with base map
L_{0}\times c\simeq L_{0}
and pullback (
\mathchar 28682\relax
open subset of
W_{0}
,
\mathchar 28961\relax:=L_{0}^{-1}(\mathchar 28682\relax)
)

\displaystyle L^{*}_{\mathchar 28682\relax}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{W}(\mathchar 28682\relax)=C^{\infty}_{W_{0}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{W}^{\vee}_{*}\big)\to\mathcal{O}_{V}(\mathchar 28961\relax)=C^{\infty}_{V_{0}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{*}\big),

(2.43)

which is fully defined by
(-\circ L_{0}|_{\mathchar 28961\relax})\otimes\bar{S}\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}\big)
.

We must now prove that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})=L
is a morphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan
, i.e., that in linear coordinates it has linear coordinate pullbacks. As said above, the linear coordinate map
\textnormal{k}\colon W\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
is the product of the linear coordinate maps
\textnormal{k}_{0}\colon W_{0}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{0}}
and
\textnormal{k}_{>}\colon W_{>}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{s}}
. The first of these coordinate maps is implemented by a basis
b_{W}
of
\mathbf{W}_{0}
and its global pullback
b_{W}^{*}\colon C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{r})\to C^{\infty}_{W_{0}}(W_{0})
sends a coordinate function
y^{\ell}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{r})
to

b_{W}^{*}\big(y^{\ell}\big)=y^{\ell}\circ b_{W}=\mathchar 28940\relax_{W}^{\ell}\in C^{\infty}_{W_{0}}(W_{0}),

where
\mathchar 28940\relax_{W}
is the dual basis (observe that
b^{*}_{W}
extends the transpose of
b_{W}
viewed as vector space isomorphism). Similarly, it is clear from Proposition
2.21
that the global pullback
\flat_{W}^{-1}
of the second coordinate map sends a coordinate function
\mathchar 28945\relax_{j}^{\ell}\in{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28945\relax]]
to

\flat^{-1}_{W}\big(\mathchar 28945\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)=\mathchar 28940\relax_{j}^{\ell}\in\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{W}^{\vee}_{*}\big),

where
\big(\mathchar 28940\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)_{j,\ell}
is the dual of a basis of
\mathbf{W_{*}}
. Based on what we just said and on the statement (
2.43
), we get that the coordinate pullbacks in the linear coordinate expression of
L
are

\big(b^{*}_{V}\big)^{-1}\big(\big(b_{W}^{*}\big(y^{\ell}\big)\big)\circ L_{0}\big)=\big(b^{*}_{V}\big)^{-1}\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}_{0}\big(\mathchar 28940\relax_{W}^{\ell}\big)\big)=\big(b^{*}_{V}\big)^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{k}(\mathbf{L}_{0})^{\ell}_{k}\mathchar 28940\relax_{V}^{k}\bigg)=\sum_{k}(\mathbf{L}_{0})^{\ell}_{k}x^{k}

and

\flat_{V}\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}_{j}\big(\flat_{W}^{-1}\big(\mathchar 28945\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)\big)\big)=\flat_{V}\big(^{t}\mathbf{L}_{j}\big(\mathchar 28940\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)\big)=\flat_{V}\bigg(\sum_{k}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{\ell}_{k}\mathchar 28940\relax^{k}_{j}\bigg)=\sum_{k}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{\ell}_{k}\mathchar 28952\relax_{j}^{k},

where the notations are self-explanatory. Hence,
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})\colon\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})\to\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{W})
is a morphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan
.

Since
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})
is essentially the transpose of
\mathbf{L}
, we have defined a functor

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan

and this functor coincides on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarVec
with the functor
\mathcal{M}
that we defined earlier.

We already mentioned that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphism, say
h
, between
V=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is implemented by a basis
(b^{i}_{k})_{i,k}
of
\mathbf{V}
. Now we can explain this observation in more detail. Indeed, the basis chosen provides a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space isomorphism
\mathbf{b}\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
, hence, the image
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b})\colon\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})\to\mathcal{M}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphism (it is even an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
), say
b\colon V\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
. The diffeomorphism
b=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b})
is a special case of the map
L=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
, whose construction has been described above. It is almost obvious from the penultimate paragraph that the diffeomorphism
h
coincides with the diffeomorphism
b
. Indeed, the diffeomorphism
h
is the product of two
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphisms
\textnormal{h}_{0}\colon V_{0}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{0}}
and
\textnormal{h}_{>}\colon V_{>}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}
(see
k
in the penultimate paragraph). The same holds for
b
, which is defined as
b=b_{0}\times b_{>}
, where
b_{0}\colon V_{0}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{0}}
and
b_{>}\colon V_{>}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}
(see (
2.42
) and
(
2.43
)
). The map
\textnormal{h}_{0}
is canonically induced by the basis
(b^{0}_{k})_{k}
of
\mathbf{V}_{0}
, and so is
b_{0}\,;
hence

\textnormal{h}_{0}=b_{0}
. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphism
b_{>}
is defined by the corresponding
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra isomorphism

\bar{S}(^{t}\mathbf{b})\colon\ \bar{S}\big(\big(\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}\big)\to\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{V}_{*}^{\vee}\big),

where the source algebra is
\Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28962\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}={\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]
. As seen above, this algebra morphism is fully defined by the transposes
{}^{t}\mathbf{b}_{j}\colon(\mathbf{R}^{q_{j}})^{\vee}\to\mathbf{V}_{j}^{\vee}
and their action on the basis
(\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{j})_{\ell}
. The action is

{}^{t}\mathbf{b}_{j}\big(\mathchar 28962\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)=\mathchar 28962\relax_{j}^{\ell}\circ\mathbf{b}_{j}=\mathchar 28940\relax_{j}^{\ell},

since the image of any
v_{j}=\sum_{k}v^{k}_{j}\,b_{k}^{j}\in\mathbf{V}_{j}
by the two maps is
v_{j}^{\ell}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\bar{S}\big(^{t}\mathbf{b}\big)=\flat^{-1}.

(2.44)

This yields
b_{>}=\textnormal{h}_{>}
. Finally, we get

\displaystyle\textnormal{h}=b=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b}).

(2.45)

Vectorification functor.
In this subsection, we define the vectorification functor

\mathcal{V}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}.

If
\textsf{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
has dimension
p|\underline{q}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}):=\mathbf{L}:=\big(\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}|)\big)^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{i}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}|)\big)^{\vee}=:\bigoplus_{i}\mathbf{L}_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec},

(2.46)

where
\mathbf{L}_{i}
has dimension
q_{i}
(
q_{0}=p
). Further, in view of item
(iii)
of Remark
2.16
, if
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\textsf{L}\to\textsf{L}^{\prime}
is a morphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt LinMan
, then
{}^{t}\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}
is a degree preserving linear map

\mathcal{V}(\mathchar 28958\relax):=\mathbf{\Phi}:=\,^{t}\mathchar 28958\relax^{*}\colon\ \mathcal{V}(\textsf{L})=\big(\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}|)\big)^{\vee}\to\big(\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}^{\prime}|)\big)^{\vee}=\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}^{\prime}).

The definition of
\mathcal{V}(\mathchar 28958\relax)
implies that
\mathcal{V}
is a functor.

Compositions of the manifoldification and the vectorification functors.

(i)
We first turn our attention to
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{M}
. If

\mathbf{V}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\qquad\big(\operatorname{dim}\mathbf{V}=p|\underline{q}\big),

its image

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})=V=V_{0}\times V_{>}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\qquad\big(\operatorname{dim}V=p|\underline{q}\big)

is the product of the linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
V_{0}
and
V_{>}
. Let
\big(b^{i}_{\ell}\big)_{i,\ell}
be a basis of
\mathbf{V}
with dual
\big(\mathchar 28940\relax_{i}^{\ell}\big)_{i,\ell}
and induced
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space isomorphism
\mathbf{b}\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
(we denote the induced diffeomorphism from
V_{0}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
by
b_{0}
). As explained above, it defines a linear coordinate map

\displaystyle\textnormal{h}=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b})\colon\ V\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}

(2.47)

with pullback morphism

\textnormal{h}^{*}=(-\circ b_{0})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\flat^{-1}\colon\ C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]\to C^{\infty}_{V_{0}}(V_{0})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{*}\big)

(see (
2.45
), (
2.43
) and (
2.44
)). Using equation (
2.13
), denoting the basis of
\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}
as usual by
\big(\mathchar 28962\relax_{i}^{\ell}\big)_{i,\ell}
, and remembering the identifications (
2.10
), we thus get

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V}))

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{O}_{V}^{\operatorname{lin}}(V_{0})\big)^{\vee}=\big(\textnormal{h}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}^{\operatorname{lin}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\big)^{\vee}=\bigg(\textnormal{h}^{*}\bigg(\bigoplus_{\ell}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{0}\oplus\bigoplus_{j,\ell}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{j}\bigg)\bigg)^{\vee}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\bigoplus_{\ell}{\mathbb{R}}\,\big(\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{0}\circ b_{0}\big)\oplus\bigoplus_{j,\ell}{\mathbb{R}}\,\flat^{-1}(\mathchar 28952\relax^{\ell}_{j})\bigg)^{\vee}=\bigg(\bigoplus_{\ell}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax^{\ell}_{0}\oplus\bigoplus_{j,\ell}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax^{\ell}_{j}\bigg)^{\vee}=\mathbf{V}.

(ii)
Regarding
\mathcal{M}\circ\mathcal{V}
, recall that if

\textsf{L}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\qquad\big(\operatorname{dim}\textsf{L}=p|\underline{q}\big).

Definition (
2.46
) yields
\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L})=\mathbf{L}=\big(\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}|)\big)^{\vee}
(notice that
\mathbf{L}
denotes a vector space here, and not a linear map) and Definition (
2.41
) leads to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L}):=L:=(L_{0},\mathcal{O}_{L})
, where
L_{0}
is
\mathbf{L}_{0}=\big(\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{0}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}|)\big)^{\vee}
viewed as smooth manifold, and where
\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
(
\mathchar 28961\relax\subset L_{0}
open) is

\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)=C^{\infty}_{L_{0}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{L}_{*}^{\vee}\big)

(see (
2.41
)). If we choose a basis
\big(\mathchar 28940\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)_{j,\ell}
of
\mathbf{L}_{*}^{\vee}
, we have

\bar{S}\big(\mathbf{L}^{\vee}_{*}\big)=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{j_{1}\leq\cdots\leq j_{k}}\bigoplus_{\ell_{1}\lhd\cdots\lhd\ell_{k}}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax_{j_{1}}^{\ell_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot\mathchar 28940\relax_{j_{k}}^{\ell_{k}}=\Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax},

where
\mathchar 28939\relax_{j}^{k}\in\mathbb{N}
(resp.,
\mathchar 28939\relax_{j}^{k}\in\{0,1\}
), if
\langle\mathchar 28941\relax_{j},\mathchar 28941\relax_{j}\rangle
is even (resp., odd) (see (
2.40
)). Just as

C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,\Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\,{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}=C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{R}}\,{\mathbb{R}}[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]=C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)[[\mathchar 28952\relax]]=\mathchar 28677\relax_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\,C^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p}}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}

(
\mathchar 28682\relax\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
open) (see
[
15
]
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)=\Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\,C^{\infty}_{L_{0}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)\,\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}=\Pi_{k}\bigoplus_{j_{1}\leq\cdots\leq j_{k}}\bigoplus_{\ell_{1}\lhd\cdots\lhd\ell_{k}}C^{\infty}_{L_{0}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)\,\mathchar 28940\relax_{j_{1}}^{\ell_{1}}\odot\cdots\odot\mathchar 28940\relax_{j_{k}}^{\ell_{k}}.

(2.48)

Remark 2.23
.

Let us mention that
L
and
L
denote a priori different linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds and that our goal is to show that they do coincide.

Recall first that, for any
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifold
M
, there is a projection

\mathchar 28943\relax_{M}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{M}\to C^{\infty}_{|M|}

of
|M|
-sheaves of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebras and that
\mathchar 28943\relax_{M}
commutes with pullbacks. In particular, if
\textnormal{h}\colon\textsf{L}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is a linear coordinate map of
L
(a (linear)
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphism), its pullback is, for any open subset
|U|\subset|\textsf{L}|
, a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra isomorphism

\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}(|h|(|U|))\to\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|)

and it restricts to a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space isomorphism

\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ \mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}(|h|(|U|))\to\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L}}(|U|).

Further, as just said, we have

\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}\circ\textnormal{h}^{*}=\textnormal{h}^{*}\circ\mathchar 28943\relax_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}=(-\circ|\textnormal{h}|)\circ\mathchar 28943\relax_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}

on
\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}(|h|(|U|))
. Taking
|U|=|\textsf{L}|
and restricting the equality to degree zero linear functions

\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28941\relax_{0}}^{\operatorname{lin}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})=(\mathbf{R}^{p})^{\vee}

(see (
2.13
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}\circ\textnormal{h}^{*}=-\circ|\textnormal{h}|,

(2.49)

or, equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}=(-\circ|\textnormal{h}|)\circ(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1},

(2.50)

where
(\textnormal{h}^{*})^{-1}
is a vector space isomorphism from
(\mathbf{L}_{0})^{\vee}=\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L},\mathchar 28941\relax_{0}}^{\operatorname{lin}}(|\textsf{L}|)
to
(\mathbf{R}^{p})^{\vee}
and where
-\circ|\textnormal{h}|
is an algebra isomorphism from
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})
to
C^{\infty}(|\textsf{L}|)
. In view of the diffeomorphism
|\textnormal{h}|\colon|\textsf{L}|\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
, the smooth manifold
|\textsf{L}|
is linear. Hence, it is a finite dimensional vector space also denoted
|\textsf{L}|
and
|\textnormal{h}|
is a vector space isomorphism, whose dual
{}^{t}|\textnormal{h}|=-\circ|\textnormal{h}|
is a vector space isomorphism from
(\mathbf{R}^{p})^{\vee}\subset C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})
to
|\textsf{L}|^{\vee}
. It follows (see also equation (
2.50
)) that the canonical map
\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}
is a vector space isomorphism from
(\mathbf{L}_{0})^{\vee}
to
|\textsf{L}|^{\vee}
. When identifying these vector spaces, we get
\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}=\operatorname{id}
and
|\textsf{L}|=\mathbf{L}_{0}
, hence the corresponding linear manifolds do also coincide:
|\textsf{L}|=L_{0}
.

To prove that the linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-manifolds
L
and
L
coincide, it now suffices to show that their function sheaves coincide. The pullback of
h
is an isomorphism
\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}
of sheaves of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebras. Since
\textnormal{h}^{*}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space isomorphism

\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ \big(\mathbf{R}^{0|\underline{q}}\big)^{\vee}=\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\to\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{lin}}_{\textsf{L},*}(|\textsf{L}|)=\mathbf{L}_{*}^{\vee},

the images
\big(\textnormal{h}^{*}\big(\mathchar 28962\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)\big)_{j,\ell}
are a basis
\big(\mathchar 28940\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big)_{j,\ell}
of
\mathbf{L}_{*}^{\vee}
. Moreover, we know that

|\textnormal{h}|=\big(\dots,\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}\big(\textnormal{h}^{*}\!\big(\mathchar 28962\relax_{0}^{\ell}\big)\big),\dots\big)=\big(\dots,\textnormal{h}^{*}\!\big(\mathchar 28962\relax_{0}^{\ell}\big),\dots\big),

as
\mathchar 28943\relax_{\textsf{L}}=\operatorname{id}
on
(\mathbf{L}_{0})^{\vee}
. Therefore, if
f(x)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})
, we get

\displaystyle\textnormal{h}^{*}(f(x))=f(\textnormal{h}^{*}\!(x))=f\circ\big(\dots,\textnormal{h}^{*}\!\big(\mathchar 28962\relax^{\ell}_{0}\big),\dots\big)=f\circ|\textnormal{h}|\in C^{\infty}(|\textsf{L}|).

(2.51)

Equation (
2.51
) (which generalizes equation (
2.49
)) shows that
\textnormal{h}^{*}
is an algebra isomorphism
\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p})\to C^{\infty}(|\textsf{L}|)
. Similarly, if
\mathchar 28961\relax\subset|\textsf{L}|
is open,
\mathchar 28682\relax:=|\textnormal{h}|(\mathchar 28961\relax)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
and
f(x)\in C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)
, we have

\textnormal{h}^{*}(f(x))=f\circ|\textnormal{h}||_{\mathchar 28961\relax}\in C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28961\relax),

so that

\displaystyle\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\to C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28961\relax)

(2.52)

is also an algebra isomorphism. Finally, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra isomorphism

\displaystyle\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ \Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\,\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\to\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)

(2.53)

sends any series
\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x)\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}
to

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\textnormal{h}^{*}(f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x))\big(\dots,\textnormal{h}^{*}\!\big(\mathchar 28952\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big),\dots\big)^{\mathchar 28939\relax}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\textnormal{h}^{*}(f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x))\big(\dots,\textnormal{h}^{*}\!\big(\mathchar 28962\relax_{j}^{\ell}\big),\dots\big)^{\mathchar 28939\relax}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\textnormal{h}^{*}(f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x))\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)

(see (
2.48
)). The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra morphism

\displaystyle\textnormal{h}^{*}\colon\ \Pi_{\mathchar 28939\relax}C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28682\relax)\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)

(2.54)

we get this way (notice that the targets of the arrows (
2.53
) and (
2.54
) are different) is visibly an isomorphism. Indeed, it is obviously injective, and it is surjective due to (
2.52
). It follows from (
2.53
) and (
2.54
) that
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)=\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
, for any open subset
\mathchar 28961\relax\subset|\textsf{L}|
. Since
\textnormal{h}^{*}
commutes with restrictions, the sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{L}
coincide and
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}))=\textsf{L}
. An alternative way of saying what we just said is to observe that in view of (
2.53
) every element of
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
is the image by
\textnormal{h}^{*}
of a unique series
\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x)\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}
and therefore belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
. Conversely, in view of (
2.52
) every element
\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}g_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}
(
g_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\in C^{\infty}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
) of
\mathcal{O}_{L}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
uniquely reads
\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}\textnormal{h}^{*}(f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x))\mathchar 28940\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}
, is therefore the image by
\textnormal{h}^{*}
of
\sum_{\mathchar 28939\relax}f_{\mathchar 28939\relax}(x)\mathchar 28952\relax^{\mathchar 28939\relax}
and so belongs to
\mathcal{O}_{\textsf{L}}(\mathchar 28961\relax)
.

(iii)
We leave it to the reader to check that both functors,
\mathcal{V}\circ\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}\circ\mathcal{V}
, coincide also on morphisms with the identity functors.

Theorem 2.24
.

The functors

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\rightleftarrows{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\ \,{:}\,\mathcal{V}

are an isomorphism of categories.

Comparison of the functors of points.
Since
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
, the fully faithful functors of points
\mathcal{F}
(see Proposition
2.8
) and
\mathcal{S}
(see Theorem
2.20
) of these categories should coincide. However, up till now, the functor
\mathcal{F}
is valued in
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)
, whereas the functor
\mathcal{S}
is valued in
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
. Since
\tt FAMod
is a subcategory of
\tt AMod
, the latter functor category is a subcategory of the former. Hence, if we show that the image
\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{V})
of any object
\mathbf{V}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
is a functor of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
(
\star
) and that the image
\mathcal{F}(\mathchar 28958\relax)
of any morphism
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{W}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
is a natural transforation of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
(
\ast
), we can conclude that
\mathcal{F}
is a functor

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big).

We start proving (
\star
). Since
\tt FAMod
is a subcategory of
\tt AMod
, we just have to show that the image

\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{V})(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)=(\mathchar 28675\relax\otimes\mathbf{V})_{0}

of any object
\mathchar 28675\relax
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}}
is a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module (
\bullet
) and that the image

\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{V})(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})=\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})=(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\otimes\mathbbmss{1}_{\mathbf{V}})_{0}

of any morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Alg
is a morphism of
\tt FAMod
(
\circ
).

To prove (
\bullet
), we consider a basis of
\mathbf{V}
(
\dim\mathbf{V}=p|\underline{q}
), i.e., an isomorphism
\mathbf{b}\colon\mathbf{V}\rightleftarrows\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\colon\mathbf{b}^{-1}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces. Since
\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{b})=\mathbf{b}_{-}
is a natural isomorphism of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)
, any of its
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components is an isomorphism

\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon\ \mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\rightleftarrows\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)\ \,{:}\,\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}

of
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-modules. We use this isomorphism to transfer to
\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
the Fréchet vector space structure of

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\big(\mathchar 28675\relax\otimes\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)_{0}=\bigoplus_{i}\bigoplus_{k}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}=\Pi_{i}\Pi_{k}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}=\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}^{\times p}\times\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{1}}^{\times q_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{N}}^{\times q_{N}}

(2.55)

(see proof of Proposition
2.17
and equation (
2.16
)), thus obtaining a well-defined Fréchet structure and making
\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
a Fréchet vector space isomorphism, i.e., a continuous linear map with continuous inverse. Since
\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
is
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear, the action
\cdot
of
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
on
\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
is related to its action
\triangleleft
on
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
by

\textrm{a}\cdot\textrm{v}=\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}(\textrm{a}\triangleleft\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\textrm{v})),

for any
\textrm{a}\in\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
and any
\textrm{v}\in\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
. The action
\cdot
is thus the composite of the continuous maps
\mathop{\rm id}\times\,\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
,
\triangleleft
, and
\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}^{-1}
, hence, it is itself continuous. The
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module and the Fréchet vector space structures on
\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
therefore define a Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure on
\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
and
\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
becomes an isomorphism of Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-modules (for any basis
\mathbf{b}
of
\mathbf{V}
).

As concerns (
\circ
), recall that
\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is a
(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})_{0}
-linear map, where the algebra morphism
(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})_{0}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}_{0}
is the restriction of
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}
. Observe now that, in view of (
2.55
), we have

\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})=(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\otimes\mathbbmss{1})_{0}=\Pi_{i}\Pi_{k}\,\mathchar 28967\relax^{*},

so that
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is continuous as product of continuous maps (indeed, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Alg
-morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}
is continuous as pullback of the associated
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism). As
\mathbf{b}_{-}
is a natural transformation of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)
, we have

\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})=\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})\circ\mathbf{b}_{\mathchar 28675\relax},

so that
\mathbf{V}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})
is continuous (and
(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})_{0}
-linear), hence, is a morphism of
\tt FAMod
.

It remains to show that (
\ast
) holds. We know that
\mathcal{F}(\mathchar 28958\relax)=\mathchar 28958\relax_{-}
is a natural transformation of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)
, i.e., its
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components
\mathchar 28958\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}
are
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-linear maps and the naturality condition is satisfied. It thus suffices to explain that
\mathchar 28958\relax_{\mathchar 28675\relax}=(\mathbbmss{1}\otimes\mathchar 28958\relax)_{0}
is continuous. Since
\mathchar 28675\relax
is a Fréchet algebra, it is a locally convex topological vector space (
LCTVS
) and
\mathbbmss{1}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax\to\mathchar 28675\relax
is a degree zero continuous linear map. Further, since
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{W}
are finite dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces, the degree zero linear map
\mathchar 28958\relax\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{W}
is automatically continuous for the canonical
LCTVS
structures on its source and target. It follows that
\mathbbmss{1}\otimes\mathchar 28958\relax
and
(\mathbbmss{1}\otimes\mathchar 28958\relax)_{0}
are continuous linear maps.

Proposition 2.25
.

The functor

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)

is fully faithful.

Proof.

The result is obvious in view of Proposition
2.8
, since
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
is a subcategory of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt AMod}\big)
.
∎

We are now ready to refine the idea expressed at the beginning of this subsection that the (fully faithful) functors of points

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)

(see Proposition
2.25
) and

\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},\tt FAMod\big)

(see Theorem
2.20
) of the isomorphic categories

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\rightleftarrows{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}\ \,{:}\,\mathcal{V}\

should coincide.

Theorem 2.26
.

The functors

\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{M},\,\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)

are naturally isomorphic.

We first prove the theorem in the Cartesian case

\mathcal{M}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\rightleftarrows{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt CarMan\ \,{:}\,\mathcal{V}

(see Proposition
2.5
). More precisely, it follows from Proposition
2.25
and Theorem
2.20
that the functors
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{S}
are (fully faithful) functors

\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)

and

\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarMan}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big).

Actually:

Proposition 2.27
.

The functors

\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{M},\,\mathcal{F}\colon\ {\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt CarVec}\to{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)

are naturally isomorphic.

Proof.

In order to construct a natural isomorphism
\textsf{I}\colon\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{F}
, we must define, for any
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
, a natural isomorphism

\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}\colon\ \mathcal{S}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\to\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)

of
{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big)
that is natural in
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
. To build
\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}
, we have to define, for each
\mathchar 28675\relax
, an isomorphism

\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon\ \mathcal{S}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(\mathchar 28675\relax)\to\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)(\mathchar 28675\relax)

of Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-modules that is natural in
\mathchar 28675\relax
. Recalling that the source and target of this arrow are

{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}},{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\qquad\big({\mathbb{R}}^{0|\underline{m}}\simeq\mathchar 28675\relax\big)

and

\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)=\big(\mathchar 28675\relax\otimes\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)_{0}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}\otimes\mathbf{R}e^{i}_{k}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}\bigoplus_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}=\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}^{\times p}\times\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{1}}^{\times q_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathchar 28675\relax_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{N}}^{\times q_{N}},

respectively, we set

\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax}\colon\ \mathrm{x}\mapsto\sum_{i,k}\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u^{k}_{i}\big)\otimes e^{i}_{k}=\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u^{k}_{i}\big)\big)=\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(x^{k}\big),\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(\mathchar 28952\relax^{k}_{j}\big)\big)=:\big(x^{k}_{\mathchar 28675\relax},\mathchar 28952\relax^{k}_{j,\mathchar 28675\relax}\big),

where
\big(u^{k}_{i}\big)=\big(x^{k},\mathchar 28952\relax^{k}_{j}\big)
are the coordinates of
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
and where
\big(e^{i}_{k}\big)_{i,k}
is the canonical basis of
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
. Since we actually used this
1:1
correspondence to transfer the Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module structure from
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
(see (
2.20
)), the bijection
\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax}
is an isomorphism of Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-modules. This isomorphism is natural with respect to
\mathchar 28675\relax
. Indeed, if
\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\colon\mathchar 28675\relax\to\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-algebra map (with corresponding
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathchar 28967\relax
), we have

\displaystyle\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*})(\mathrm{x})\big)=\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax^{\prime}}(\mathrm{x}\circ\mathchar 28967\relax)=\big(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(x^{k}\big)\big),\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(\mathchar 28952\relax^{k}_{j}\big)\big)\big)=(\mathchar 28967\relax^{*}\otimes\mathbbmss{1})_{0}\big(\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathrm{x})\big).

It now suffices to check that
\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}
is natural with respect to
\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
. Hence, let
\mathbf{L}\colon\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\to\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}
be a degree zero linear map and let
L\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
be the corresponding linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-morphism
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{L})
. In order to prove that

\displaystyle\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}}\circ\mathcal{S}(L)=\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{L})\circ\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}},

(2.56)

we have to show that the
\mathchar 28675\relax
-components of these natural transformations coincide. To find that these Fréchet
\mathchar 28675\relax_{0}
-module morphisms coincide, we must explain that they associate the same image to every
\mathrm{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}(\mathchar 28675\relax)
. When denoting the coordinates of
{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}
by
\big(u^{\prime\ell}_{i}\big)=\big(x^{\prime\ell},\mathchar 28952\relax^{\prime\ell}_{j}\big)
, we obtain

\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}},\mathchar 28675\relax}\big(\mathcal{S}(L)_{\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathrm{x})\big)=\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}},\mathchar 28675\relax}(L\circ\mathrm{x})=\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(L^{*}\big(x^{\prime\ell}\big)\big),\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(L^{*}\big(\mathchar 28952\relax^{\prime\ell}_{j}\big)\big)\big)=\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(L^{*}\big(u^{\prime\ell}_{i}\big)\big)\big),

where

L^{*}(u^{\prime\ell}_{i})=\sum_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathbf{L}^{\ell i}_{ik}\,u^{k}_{i},

in view of (
2.11
) and (
2.12
). It follows that

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(L^{*}\big(u^{\prime\ell}_{i}\big)\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{q_{i}}\mathbf{L}^{\ell i}_{ik}\,\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u^{k}_{i}\big)\bigg)=\sum_{i,\ell}\bigg(\sum_{k}\mathbf{L}^{\ell i}_{ik}\,\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u_{i}^{k}\big)\bigg)\otimes e^{\prime i}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,k}\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u_{i}^{k}\big)\otimes\bigg(\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{L}^{\ell i}_{ik}e^{\prime i}_{\ell}\bigg)=\sum_{i,k}\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u_{i}^{k}\big)\otimes\mathbf{L}\big(e^{i}_{k}\big)

\displaystyle=(\mathbbmss{1}\otimes\mathbf{L})_{0}\bigg(\sum_{i,k}\mathrm{x}^{*}\big(u_{i}^{k}\big)\otimes e^{i}_{k}\bigg)=\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{L})_{\mathchar 28675\relax}\big(\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathchar 28675\relax}(\mathrm{x})\big),

where
\big(e^{\prime i}_{\ell}\big)_{i,\ell}
is the basis of
\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}
.
∎

We are now able to prove Theorem
2.26
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we set

{\tt T}:={\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big).

In order to build a natural isomorphism
\mathcal{I}\colon\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{F}
, we must define, for any
\mathbf{V}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
, a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{V}}\colon\mathcal{S}(V)\to\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{V})

of
{\tt T}
that is natural in
\mathbf{V}
.
Set

\dim\mathbf{V}=p|\underline{q}

and let
\mathbf{b}
be a basis of
\mathbf{V}
, or, equivalently, a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector space isomorphism
\mathbf{b}\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}
. In view of (
2.47
), the morphism
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b})\colon V\to{\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}
is a linear
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-diffeomorphism. Using Proposition
2.25
and Theorem
2.20
, we obtain that

\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{b})\colon\ \mathcal{F}(\mathbf{V})\to\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)

and

\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b}))\colon\ \mathcal{S}(V)\to\mathcal{S}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)

are natural isomorphisms of
{\tt T}
. As

\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}\colon\ \mathcal{S}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)\to\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\big)

is a natural isomorphism of
{\tt T}
as well, the transformation

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{V}}:=\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{b}^{-1}\big)\circ\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}\circ\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b}))

is a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{V}}\colon\ \mathcal{S}(V)\to\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{V})

as requested. In view of equation (
2.56
), the transformation
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{V}}
is well-defined, i.e., is independent of the basis chosen.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{V}}
is natural in
\mathbf{V}
, i.e., that, for any degree zero linear map
\phi\colon\mathbf{V}\to\mathbf{W}
(
\dim\mathbf{W}=r|\underline{s}
) and for any basis
\mathbf{b}
(resp.,
\mathbf{c}
) of
\mathbf{V}
(resp.,
\mathbf{W}
), we have

\mathcal{F}(\phi)\circ\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{b}^{-1}\big)\circ\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}\circ\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{b}))=\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{c}^{-1}\big)\circ\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}}\circ\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{c}))\circ\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{M}(\phi)),

or, equivalently,

\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}}\circ\mathcal{S}\big(\mathcal{M}\big(\mathbf{c}\circ\phi\circ\mathbf{b}^{-1}\big)\big)=\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbf{c}\circ\phi\circ\mathbf{b}^{-1}\big)\circ\textsf{I}_{\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}}.

Since
\mathbf{L}:=\mathbf{c}\circ\phi\circ\mathbf{b}^{-1}
is a degree zero linear map
\mathbf{L}\colon\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}}\to\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}
, equation (
2.56
) allows once more to conclude.
∎

Internal Homs
. A topological property is a property of topological spaces that is invariant under homeomorphisms (isomorphisms of topological spaces). More intuitively, a “topological property” is a property that only depends on the topological structure, or, equivalently, that can be expressed by means of open subsets. Similarly, equivalences of categories (“isomorphisms” of categories) preserve all “categorical properties and concepts”. Hence, an equivalence should preserve products. It turns out that this statement is actually correct. More precisely, if
\mathcal{E}\colon{\tt S}\to{\tt T}
is part of an equivalence of categories, then a functor
\mathcal{D}\colon{\tt I}\to{\tt S}
has limit
s
if and only if the functor
\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{D}\colon{\tt I}\to{\tt T}
has limit
\mathcal{E}(s)
. Applying the statement to the discrete index category
{\tt I}
with two objects
\{1,2\}
and setting
\mathcal{D}(i)=s_{i}
(
i\in\{1,2\}
), we get that
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
have product
s
if and only if
\mathcal{E}(s_{1})
and
\mathcal{E}(s_{2})
have product
\mathcal{E}(s)
. Now, the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
has the obvious binary product
\times
. It follows that, for any vector spaces
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
, the manifolds
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V}),\,\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{W})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
have product

\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})\times\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{W})=\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V}\times\mathbf{W}).

If
\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
, the categorical isomorphism implies that
\textsf{L}=\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}))
and similarly for
\textsf{L}^{\prime}
, so that the product
\textsf{L}\times\textsf{L}^{\prime}
exists and is

\displaystyle\textsf{L}\times\textsf{L}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L})\times\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}^{\prime})).

(2.57)

Hence, the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
has finite products.

Equation (
2.57
) shows that we got the product of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
by transferring to
{\tt T}:={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
the product of
{\tt S}:={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
. We can similarly transfer to
{\tt T}
the closed symmetric monoidal structure of
{\tt S}
. Indeed, the category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec}
is closed symmetric monoidal for the standard tensor product
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}-
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces and the standard internal Hom
\underline{\text{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}(-,-)
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-vector spaces, which is defined, on objects for instance, by

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}):=\bigoplus_{i}\underline{\text{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec},\mathchar 28941\relax_{i}}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec},

for any
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec
. Of course, if
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}\in\tt S
, then
\underline{\text{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})\in\tt S
, and the same holds for
\mathbf{V}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\mathbf{W}
. It follows that
{\tt S}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt FinVec}
is also a closed symmetric monoidal category. If we set now

\displaystyle\textsf{L}\otimes_{\tt T}\textsf{L}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Vec}\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}^{\prime})),\qquad\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\tt T}(\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime}):=\mathcal{M}\big(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec}}(\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}),\mathcal{V}(\textsf{L}^{\prime}))\big),

(2.58)

and similarly for morphisms, we get a closed symmetric monoidal structure on
{\tt T}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
:

Proposition 2.28
.

The category
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt LinMan}
is closed symmetric monoidal for the structure (
2.58
).

Alternatively, we could have defined
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\tt T}(\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime})\in\tt T
using the fully faithful functor of points

\mathcal{S}\colon\ {\tt T}\ni\textsf{L}\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Man}}(-,\textsf{L})=:\textsf{L}(-)\in{\tt Fun}_{0}\big({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Pts}^{\operatorname{op}},{\tt FAMod}\big),

i.e., defining first a functor
F_{\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime}}(-)
in the target category, and then showing that this functor is representable by some
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\tt T}(\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime})\in\tt T
:

F_{\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime}}(-)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}\tt Man}(-,\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\tt T}(\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime}))=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\tt T}(\textsf{L},\textsf{L}^{\prime})(-).

This “functor of points approach” is often easier.

To shed some light on our more abstract definition above, we now compute
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\tt T}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{p|\underline{q}},{\mathbb{R}}^{r|\underline{s}}\big)(\mathchar 28675\relax)
(
\diamond
) assuming some familiarity with
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}
-graded matrices
\operatorname{gl}(r|\underline{s}\times p|\underline{q},\mathchar 28675\relax)
with entries in
\mathchar 28675\relax\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Alg}
. Details can be found in Section
3.1
which we leave in its natural place. However, we highly recommend reading it before working though the end of this section.

We observe first that

\underline{\Hom}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{n}{\tt Vec},\mathchar 28941\relax_{k}}\big(\mathbf{R}^{p|\underline{q}},\mathbf{R}^{r|\underline{s}}\big)=\operatorname{gl}_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{k}}\big(r|\underline{s}\times p|\underline{q},\,{\mathbb{R}}\big)\in{\tt Vec}.

In order to understand the gist here, we consider the case
n=2
, so that a matrix
X\in\operatorname{gl}_{\mathchar 28941\relax_{k}}\big(r|\underline{s}\times p|\underline{q},\,{\mathbb{R}}\big)
